# Validating symphony-protocol: the harness design

This is the design of the harness that validates this library against its spec: scripted scenarios, an exhaustive small-scope checker, a mutation lane that tests the checker, standard-library property lanes, CrossHair run by hand, and a monthly canary on live GitHub. It is the one document a later session builds the harness from. It joins a first design and an adversarial critique of that design, both written on 2026-10-13 in a session's scratch space: the critique's corrections are applied in place, and the prototype both of them measured is kept in [prototype/](prototype/), whose README says how to run each script and what it printed.

- **Line numbers** in this repository's files refer to commit cf3bdb0, where the package (`sdlc_protocol/`, version 0.4.0) was last changed by a996ec9. They will move as the package changes; the function names beside them will not. Line numbers in `validation/prototype/` refer to its files as this branch adds them.
- **Other repositories' files** are named with the repository: symphony-template's `.github/workflows/board.yml` (read at a1d9695), coordbench's `PARITY.md` and the other coordbench files section 1 names (read at d6a3480; PARITY.md was last changed by e8e72f7), beethoven-qa's `src/beethoven_qa/gate.py` (read at 85f0e1c), symphony-app's `manifest.json` (read at 17cea0c), and GitHub's documentation by its path in the github/docs repository, read on 2026-10-13.
- **Bare module names.** A name such as claims.py or adapters/github.py is a file of the package, under `sdlc_protocol/`; a bare name that follows a path in the same list, as measure.py does in "validation/prototype/explore.py and measure.py", is a file in that path's folder. Paths inside a scenario's own repository (`src/1.py`, `docs/agents/claims/1.md`) and REST paths are not files of this one.
- **Planned files.** Build step 1 of section 14 is built: validation/model.py, fake_github.py, cycle.py, verbs.py, policies.py, oracle.py, invariants.py, explore.py and page.py, GUARANTEES.md, and their tests, tests/test_validation_*.py. The files the later steps will add, and the folder `validation-out/` their lanes write to, are listed in section 12 and are marked "planned" where they are named; every other path in this document names a file that exists at this commit.
- **The package changes only by the owner's decisions.** F1, F3, F5, F6, F10 and F11 are fixed in 0.4.1 (pull request #11, released and synced on 2026-10-13); the owner decided every open item on 2026-10-13 (section 14) and the fixes land one pull request each, all after 0.4.1: F7 first (the guard reads the claim file before a lease), then F12 ("main moved" by the state on any run), F4 (one look deals the whole run), F14 (every open, unclaimed issue a worker's label names shown under Given to workers), F9 (a draft, or a pull request a rule holds, read through mergeable and one compare into a standing the board reads first, so it hears "main moved"), F13 (the oldest overlapping work, by when GitHub says the push that made its branch came, lands first; found by build step 1 when its oracle was held to the spec's words), F15 (a release puts todo on an issue that carries none of todo, epic and blocker, so a task a worker's label alone gave is listed in the Queue again, and a worker takes an unclaimed blocker, which the board lists under Blockers, before the Queue; found by the review of F14's fix), F17 (a pull request from a fork is no claim's, whatever its branch is called, so it neither finishes nor holds a claim, and the board lists it, tells it and counts it as work of its own; found by the review of F13's fix), F18 (the clerk reads whose a claim is from its claim file, the labels only where no file names a worker, and deals a task the labels of several workers give to the first of them with nothing else to do; found by the fifth review of F15's fix), F19 (a pull request someone who may not write opens from a claim's own branch is no claim's, as a fork's is not, so it neither finishes nor holds the claim, and a release's rename closes it with the claim's own; found by the review of F17's fix) and F2 (a claim and the pull requests from its branch are one piece, named by its issue in the overlap markers and told on that issue, a marker on the claim's own pull request counting as one on the issue, so opening a pull request tells nothing again, and a marker counts for a claim only from the push that made its branch, since an issue's number names each claim on it in turn; the issue staying the work's identity for the whole claim, as the coordinator's checkpoint of 2026-10-14 records the choice); F16, found by the review of F9's fix, is no item of that decision and is settled in the spec rather than the library: the spec now says a pull request GitHub calls clean, unstable or has_hooks is not held for being behind and hears nothing, as the library already does, the answer the owner chose on 2026-10-14 ("Follow GitHub"). F8, decided with the rest on 2026-10-13 and built once canary C5 showed on 2026-10-15 that GitHub's activity records a push with GitHub's own time, is fixed last: a claim's last commit is when GitHub received the push that set its head, the committer date standing in only where the activity, read once more after a pause where the first read lists no such push or GitHub gives it no answer that holds, records no such push or GitHub will not read it, and a branch pushed again at a head a release kept is released by that release. F20, found by the review of build step 2 (symphony-protocol#16) and fixed after F8, raises the version to 0.5.0: a run whose release comment GitHub refused, and the read of the issue's comments after it too, leaves the labels as the release left them and counts no release, and the next run settles it, where until the fix it put them back beside a comment that might stand, which held the claim for good. F21, found by the review behind the taxonomy of 2026-10-15 and fixed after 0.5.0, so carried by the release after it: the push guard judges each push in each clone it may run in: where `git -C`, `env -C` or a `cd` before it sends it, a cd that fails leaving it where the shell stood, and, after a move, where it cannot tell the clone, where a `~` names it or where a variable names the repository, also where the command starts or in the session's own clone, where 0.5.0's guard judged every push, so a lease onto another worker's claim from a clone that has fetched it is refused there too. Every push is judged in the session's own clone, as by 0.5.0's guard, save one whose clone git's or env's -C names by an absolute path, or a relative one with no cd, pushd or popd before it, holding nothing the shell expands, and whose repository nothing names, which is judged where the -C names alone (section 13, F21, says where that is not where the push runs). F22, found by the same review and fixed after 0.5.0, so carried by the release after it too: an issue reopened once its claim ended kept the labels that claim left, claimed, in-review and its worker's, so with no branch standing for it the board listed it nowhere and no worker was dealt it, and with its merged claim branch kept, in-review kept that branch's claim from ever being released; the coordinator, in the board's run before it releases, now frees an open issue GitHub says was reopened that carries claimed or in-review, unless a pull request marked ready from its claim branch finishes it: with no claim branch and no released branch standing, todo goes on as a release puts it on, then every worker's label, in-review and claimed come off, claimed last; with one standing, in-review alone comes off, so the claim is released once its branch has gone a day without a commit, in the same run where it has. F26, found by the fourth review of F21's fix and fixed after 0.5.0, so carried by the release after it too: the guard reads a wrapper's long option as GNU's getopt_long does, by any start of its name that names it alone, with a value only where the option needs one, so the push behind `env --uns HOME`, `nice --adj 5` or `timeout --sig KILL 60` is judged, and one behind `env --ch ../other` is judged in ../other, where until the fix the word after each was read as the command and the push behind it passed. F23, the residual the second review of F20's fix left, is fixed after 0.5.0, whose version it keeps: a run whose release comment GitHub answered with a server error, with no answer at all or, since its review, with a rate limit's refusal, and whose read of the issue's comments after it lists none, cannot tell whether GitHub wrote the comment and lists it only later, so it leaves the labels as the release left them, as where it cannot read the comments, and puts them back only after a refusal that says GitHub wrote nothing, a 4xx other than a rate limit's, where until the fix it put them back after any error, and a comment GitHub had written stood beside them, which held the claim for good. F25, found in the reviews after F21's fix and fixed after 0.5.0, so carried by the release after it too: the guard reads a command more than one way and judges every push any reading finds, whole with a quote that never closes read as a character of its word, and with its here-documents' bodies cut out but for the substitutions bash runs of them, so a push bash runs before a line it cannot read, or between two bodies whose apostrophes the guard read as quotes, is judged, where until the fix such a command hid every push in it. F27, finding 4 of F23's first review, is fixed after 0.5.0 too: a release that fails taking a label off puts back the label whose removal failed with those it took off, unless GitHub refused it with a 4xx other than a rate limit's, where until the fix a label GitHub took off and lost its answer to stayed off a held claim's issue. F28, found by the adversarial review of F26's fix, is fixed after 0.5.0 too: after env's options the guard reads every word that holds a = as an assignment, as env does, a lone - as -i, as GNU env does, and the string env's -S gives as the words env splits it into, and env NAME=value with no command as setting nothing in the shell, so the push behind `env 1x=2`, `env -- -` or inside `env -S '...'` is judged, where until the fix the word was read as the command and the push passed. No finding is read as built now, known.json's as_built being empty since F2's fix, and no finding is open since F26's, F23's, F25's, F27's and F28's.
- **Where the numbers come from.** Every number was measured in one container: 4 CPUs shared with other sessions, Python 3.12.3, git 2.43.0. State counts are exact and were reproduced on this branch; times move with the load.

## Summary

The harness runs the library's own code, never a copy of it:
- **The board side.** `github.collect`, `coordinate`, `release_claims` and `post_comments` run against a fake of GitHub's REST API that keeps state. The fake plugs in through the `opener` argument the adapter already takes (adapters/github.py:112-118), and it is faithful to the transport under it: a request line that is not ASCII fails in the fake as `http.client` makes it fail live.
- **The merge gate** runs over the library's own MemoryWorld and BareGitWorld.
- **Workers are scripts, not Claude.** They act in an in-memory model and, for two scenarios and every counterexample, in a real bare git repository, where git itself refuses the second push. A clock that only the scenario moves drives both.
- **Judging.** An oracle written from the text of spec/PROTOCOL.md, importing nothing from the package, computes from the state before each board run what that run must do. The run's actual effects (comments by kind and target, labels taken off, renames, deletions, in their order) must equal the oracle's; the run must never raise over a refused write or a branch name; and a second run with nothing new must make no write call.

There are five lanes:
1. **Fast**, inside the required check `check`, adding at most 60 seconds: scenarios S01 to S05 in memory (S01 and S05 also in git), one regression trace per finding, three table-driven tests (the gate's presets, the protocol check, the clerk's planning), the checker at depth 5 to 6 plus searches started from scenario prefixes, the guard grammar judged by git's recorded answers, the dealing search, and a property lane over the Unicode classes Python's own `str` methods define.
2. **Mutation**, the harness's acceptance test: 10 to 20 mutants of the library's rules, each of which the fast lane's searches must catch.
3. **Deep**, weekly and by hand: two issues at depth 7, one issue at depth 8, a seeded soak, every counterexample and a sample of paths replayed in git, git's answers for the whole guard grammar, and the gate over BareGitWorld.
4. **CrossHair**, by hand from a scratch virtual environment, over the same assert-shaped properties, with no dependency in CI.
5. **Canary**, monthly and by hand, for the few things only live GitHub shows, on the canary repository OpenSpineConsortium/symphony-protocol-canary.

The prototype and its critique found twelve things, build step 1 two more, the reviews of F14's, F9's, F13's and F17's fixes one more each, the fifth review of F15's fix one more, the review of build step 2 one more, and the review behind the taxonomy of 2026-10-15 two more. Section 13 has the evidence for each and how to reproduce it.

| | Finding | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | A release whose label removal failed is never completed: the claim stays held for good | fixed in 0.4.1 |
| F2 | Overlapping work is told again when a claim opens its pull request | fixed after 0.4.1 (section 13) |
| F3 | The push guard passes forced pushes and pushes to main in plain shapes: a `+` refspec, bundled short options (`-fu`), abbreviations git accepts (`--al`, `--mirr`), a destination written `heads/main`, and any command behind `VAR=value`, `env`, `command`, `nice` or `time` | fixed in 0.4.1 |
| F4 | The clerk can deal one issue to two workers of the same run | fixed after 0.4.1: one look deals the whole run (team.deals, `team deal`), which the workers' workflow takes in its first job by the template's own pull request |
| F5 | Two branch names can claim one issue (`claude/03` beside `claude/3`) | fixed in 0.4.1 |
| F6 | One hand-made branch name stops every board run: `claude/\u00b2` in the render, and any non-ASCII name, `claude/caf\u00e9` included, in a live run | fixed in 0.4.1 |
| F7 | A worker that has fetched can take someone else's claim with `--force-with-lease` | fixed after 0.4.1: the guard reads the claim file before a lease (guard.other_holder) |
| F8 | Idleness is measured by the committer date, which the person pushing sets | fixed after 0.4.1: a claim's last commit is when GitHub received the push that set its head, by the repository's activity (github.head_since, board.Release.last_commit), an entry that set the head dating it, and any other, whatever heads it names, when it came after the run began; a first read that dates no push, or that GitHub gives no answer that holds, is made once more after a pause, and the committer date stands in where the second dates none either or GitHub refuses the read for good, but never earlier than the newest entry either read listed, which the run's log says; a branch pushed again at a head a release kept is released by that release whenever it came, before that push or after (board.release_counts), and released again only where the board counts no release of it (coordinator.kept_released, by board.is_released); in the run a branch's push started, a head no read dates, or one a read dates by an older push of the same head, is dated at the run's start (cli.pushed_branch) |
| F9 | A draft pull request may never be told "main moved" | fixed after 0.4.1: the adapter reads an opaque state, blocked or draft, through mergeable and one compare into a standing the board reads first (github.standing_of, board.Pull.standing), so a draft or a held pull request hears "main moved"; a branch GitHub calls clean while it is behind is F16 |
| F10 | Releasing a finished draft by hand never takes effect | fixed in 0.4.1 |
| F11 | One refused comment fails the whole board run, and the board branch is not written | fixed in 0.4.1 |
| F12 | "Main moved" is lost when the board's concurrency group replaces the run the push to main started | fixed after 0.4.1: the word depends on the state, and the copies' board.yml queues runs (`queue: max`) |
| F13 | The Overlaps' landing order reads "the oldest" as the lowest number, an issue's and a pull request's alike, so a claim made later lands before an older draft | fixed after 0.4.1: the oldest is the work that began first, by the time GitHub records for the push that made its branch (board.landing_key, github.began_times), read for the overlapping work alone, work nothing dates last |
| F14 | Given to workers needs the label todo and leaves epics out, which the spec's sentence does not say, so an issue labelled only worker:<name> is shown nowhere | fixed after 0.4.1: every open, unclaimed issue a worker's label names is shown, whatever else it is labelled (board.given_rows), and the clerk takes a given task with or without todo |
| F15 | A released task that a worker's label alone gave is shown nowhere: the release takes the worker's label off, and the Queue lists only an issue labelled todo | fixed after 0.4.1: a release puts todo on an issue that carries none of todo, epic and blocker (coordinator.labels_put_on), and a release that fails takes it off again (github.put_labels_on, github.put_back); a released blocker is listed under Blockers, and the spec's Pick has a worker take an unclaimed one before the Queue |
| F16 | A pull request GitHub calls clean while its branch is behind main, as under a rule that does not require an up-to-date branch or under none, never hears "main moved", which the spec's words owed it | fixed in the spec after 0.4.1: such a pull request may merge as it stands under the repository's rules and hears nothing, as the library as built does (spec, Work in the open; github.standing_of) |
| F17 | A pull request from a fork whose branch bears a claim branch's name is taken for that claim's: marked ready it keeps the claim from ever being released, and the board names it on the claim's row, folds it into the claim's work in the Overlaps, and leaves it off the page and silent once the claim is released | fixed after 0.4.1: a pull request from a fork is no claim's, whatever its branch is called (board.claim_of), as the coordinator, the board, the adapter's reading of releases (github.ready_branches, github.releases) and the clerk's of a claim's pull request (github.pull_detail) read it |
| F18 | The clerk reads whose a claim is, and whose a task is, from the issue's labels alone: a worker whose label an issue carries is told to carry another worker's claim, a claim whose issue no label or two labels name is carried by nobody, and a task the labels of two workers give is dealt to neither | fixed after 0.4.1: a claim is the worker's its claim file names, the labels deciding only where no file names one (team.Snapshot.holder), as a release names whose it was, and a task several workers' labels give goes to the first of them in the team's order with nothing else to do, after each one's own claims and any task given to it alone (team.moves_from) |
| F19 | A pull request anyone who may read the repository opens from a claim's own branch, claude/<n> here, is taken for the claim's: marked ready it keeps the claim from ever being released, said finished as a draft it holds the claim, and the board names it on the claim's row and folds it into the claim's work | fixed after 0.4.1: a claim's pull request is one a writer opened, by GitHub's author_association, as an App, or by the collaborator permission API (claims.opened_by_writer); the adapter marks a stranger's (github.permissions, may_open, mark_strangers), every reading goes through board.claim_of, and a release's rename closes it with the claim's own (board.closed_by_release) |
| F20 | A release whose comment GitHub refused, its answer lost or the comment never written, and whose read of the issue's comments after it GitHub refused too, puts the labels back beside a comment that may stand, which holds the claim for good | fixed after 0.4.1: such a run leaves the labels as the release left them and counts no release, and the next run, reading the comments, settles it as it settles any claim whose labels are off (github.landed_release, github.release_one, github.unsettled) |
| F21 | The push guard judges every push in the session's own clone: a push that `git -C`, `env -C` or a `cd` before it sends from another clone is judged by the session's branches and claim files, so a lease onto another worker's claim from a clone that has fetched it passes where the session's own clone has not fetched it; and `git --config-env`, `git --shallow-file` or an empty `git -C ''` hides a push from the guard altogether | fixed after 0.5.0: each push is judged in each clone it may run in, its branches and claim files read there: where git's and env's -C and the shell's moves send it, where the command starts when a move came before it, and the session's own clone where either cannot be told, where a variable names the repository, or where a relative -C follows a move (guard.refused_push, guard.push_places, guard.placed_commands, guard.push_place) |
| F22 | An issue reopened once its claim ended keeps the labels that claim left, claimed, in-review and its worker's: with no branch standing for it the board lists it in no section and the clerk deals it to nobody, and where the repository kept the merged claim branch, in-review keeps that branch's claim from ever being released | fixed after 0.5.0: the coordinator, in the board's run before it releases, frees an open issue GitHub's state_reason says was reopened that carries claimed or in-review, unless a pull request marked ready from its claim branch finishes it; with no claim branch and no released branch standing, todo on where a release would put it, then every worker's label, in-review and claimed off, claimed last and nothing put back; with one standing, in-review alone off, so the claim is released once due, in the same run where it is, a released branch standing only where its release, or the label claimed put back later than any entry the activity lists for the claim branch since, came after the issue's last close (its reviews); the issue and its claim's pull request read again first, the claim branch too where none was read, and again before claimed comes off (coordinator.reopened_due, coordinator.freeing, github.free_reopened, github.ended_releases, github.may_be_due) |
| F23 | A release comment GitHub writes and answers with a server error or with no answer at all, and lists only after the run's read of the issue's comments, stands beside the labels the run puts back, which holds the claim for good | fixed after 0.5.0: a run whose comment GitHub answered so, and whose read of the comments lists none, leaves the labels as the release left them and counts no release, as where it cannot read them, and puts them back only after a refusal, a 4xx other than a rate limit's since its review, which says GitHub wrote nothing (github.wrote_nothing, github.not_listed, github.release_one) |
| F25 | The push guard reads a command whole or not at all: a quote that never closes hides every push in the command, those bash runs before the line it cannot read among them, and a here-document's body is read with an ordinary line's quoting, so an apostrophe in one body and another in a later body pair around the push between them, the shape a session writes a commit message, a push and a pull request's body in | fixed after 0.5.0: the hook reads each command whole, a quote that never closes read as a character of its word (guard.plain_text, lenient), and, where it holds here-documents, with their bodies cut out but for the command substitutions of a body the shell expands (guard.readings, guard.here_document_bodies), and judges every push either reading finds (guard.placed_commands); a body is told as bash 5.2 tells it, in a substitution inside double quotes too (guard.takes_here_document), ending at a line that starts with its word and holds a ) inside a substitution (guard.closes_substitution) and at a line joined from those that end in a backslash in a body the shell expands (guard.continues), and a (( is arithmetic only where bash reads it so (guard.arithmetic_end); since its second review a body whose substitution closes on its operator's line starts at the next newline outside it (guard.leave), a case pattern's ) closes no substitution (guard.case_step), a (( counts its parentheses past quotes (guard.matching_parenthesis), and an expanded body's substitutions are read with their own bodies cut out (guard.substitutions_in), down to 16 levels, past which the command is refused unread (guard.MAX_NESTING), in a time in proportion to the text; since its third review a ) inside ${ } or backquotes closes no substitution (guard.expansion_step, guard.braced_step), every scan of a text shares its matches (guard.scan), a run of cd or pushd takes a time in proportion to it (guard.PATH_MAX, guard.stacked), and a reading past 10 seconds refuses the command unread; since its fourth review $$ opens nothing, $[ ] is read whole (guard.bracketed_step), a backquoted span is one part of its word and its text commands of their own (guard.backquoted_word), and the deadline holds the judging and git's answers too (guard.DEADLINE_SECONDS, guard.first_refusal) |
| F26 | The push guard reads a wrapper's long option only by its whole name, where GNU's env, nice, timeout and time take any start of it that names no other option: the value after `env --uns`, `nice --adj` or `timeout --sig` is read as the command, so the push behind it passes, `env --ch ../other` hides the push and `env --c=../other` the clone it runs in, and GNU time's `--output-file`, which the guard knew as `--output`, hides the push too | fixed after 0.5.0: each wrapper's long options are read as getopt_long reads them, by the exact name, else the one option a start names, with the next word as the value only where the option needs one (guard.long_option_of, guard.long_value_is_next, guard.WRAPPERS), and env's --chdir by any start of its name (guard.names_chdir) |
| F27 | A label GitHub takes off and answers with a server error or with no answer at all, as a release takes it off, is not put back with the labels the run took off before it: the release fails there and the claim stays held, while its issue lacks the label, claimed without the worker's, or the worker's without claimed, until the next run | fixed after 0.5.0: a release that fails taking a label off puts back the label whose removal failed too, unless GitHub refused it with a 4xx other than a rate limit's, which says it took nothing off (github.take_labels_off, github.wrote_nothing, github.release_one) |
| F28 | After env's options the push guard reads as an assignment only what the shell would, a name and a =, where GNU env, and the env of FreeBSD and macOS, read every word that holds a = as one; it reads the lone - GNU env takes as -i as the command, and the string env's -S splits into the command as one word; so the push behind `env 1x=2`, `env A-B=1`, `env -- -` or inside `env -S 'git push ...'` passes; and it reads `env GIT_DIR=x` with no command as GIT_DIR set in the shell, judging each later push by its words and in the session's own clone alone | fixed after 0.5.0: each word that holds a = after env's options is an assignment (guard.env_assigns, guard.past_env_assignments), a lone - there is passed (guard.past_lone_dash), -S's string is split as env.c splits it and read in its place (guard.split_string, guard.with_split_strings), and an assignment behind a wrapper other than bash's time sets nothing in the shell (guard.sets_the_shells_own) |

What held wherever the prototype looked, and how far that goes:
- Under honest workers, one issue had one holder, and a holder who came back between the release comment and the rename kept the claim.
- Every merge-gate invariant held over 21,848 to 24,920 states for each of four policies. Over MemoryWorld those invariants partly check MemoryWorld itself, so the ones that matter move to BareGitWorld (G13).
- "No work is ever lost" held only because the prototype had no verb that drops a commit. The spec allows a rebase pushed with a lease and the tidy workflow's deletions, so the guarantee is restated as the board's and the coordinator's alone (G4).
- The checker was not sensitive enough. With a sound state key it caught 1 of 8 hand-made mutants of the library, and 4 of 8 once a 24-hour wait was added (section 7). The corrections in sections 2 to 6 are what that measurement asked for.

## 1. What made the coordbench pilots convincing, and how each part carries over

| coordbench | Evidence in coordbench | The harness |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every arm runs on the same task pair, side by side | bench/cooperbench/pilot.sh:1-11 and 35-40 run solo through sdlc on one task, then the report | The scenarios' verbs drive the memory and the git world alike, and the checker uses the same verbs. |
| The score comes from CooperBench's own code, not a rewrite of it | PARITY.md row 0 (87-97): coordbench/run.py calls CooperBench's own `test_merged` | The harness drives the library's real adapter, board and gate. Only GitHub, and in the memory world git, are stood in for. |
| Every difference is stated, with a status | PARITY.md:1-9, its status words at 28-35 and its summary table at 64-79 | validation/GUARANTEES.md has one row per guarantee sentence of the spec, with a status: held, held under an assumption, contradicted (naming the finding) or not checked. Section 3.6 is the same kind of table for the worlds. |
| One measure, computed two independent ways | PARITY.md row 1 (99-122) keeps the cascade's two verdicts apart | The oracle restates the spec from its text, and its answer is compared with what the board rendered and what the run did. |
| Nothing calls a model unless the owner runs it on purpose | PARITY.md, decision 4 (1796-1806) | Workers are scripts. No token, no Claude. |
| The report is pinned by tests on synthetic inputs | tests/test_experiment_report.py | tests/test_validation_report.py (planned) pins the Markdown on a synthetic results file. |
| The command behind each measure is named, with what it costs | PARITY.md, "Producing each measure" (2026-2061) | The lane table in section 12. |

coordbench's comparison page, one self-contained file that opens offline (PARITY.md, decision 11 at 1838-1842; coordbench/compare.py), is the model for an HTML report. Here that report comes last and is optional (section 10).

## 2. The guarantees

### 2.1 What a run records

A run is a list of steps. Each step records the time, the actor, the verb, its arguments and its outcome. After each step the harness keeps:
- **Refs and commits:** each ref's commit; for each commit its parents, author, committer date and tree; and every commit that was ever the head of a claim branch.
- **Issues:** state, labels, and comments with their whole bodies (author, author_association, created_at, body). It also keeps the label events GitHub keeps on an issue's timeline: who added or took off which label, and when.
- **Pull requests:** number, head branch, author, draft or ready, open, closed or merged, updated_at, and comments with their whole bodies.
- **Commands:** every git command a worker ran, the push guard's verdict on it (guard.run, guard.py:235-255) and, in the git world, what git did.
- **Board runs:** the event that queued each run, and whether it ran or was replaced while it waited. For a run that ran: the snapshot it collected, every write call it made in order (method, path, and for a comment its kind and target), the STATE.md text, whether it raised, its exit code, and whether the board branch was written.
- **The clock.**

### 2.2 Terms, from the spec's text

Each term restates a sentence of spec/PROTOCOL.md, cited by line, over the records above. None is defined through the function under test; section 2.6 says how the oracle keeps to that.

- **claude/\<n\>**, the claim branch of issue n, spelled exactly as the spec spells it (line 29: "Push a new branch `claude/<issue number>`"): n in ASCII decimal digits, as GitHub numbers issues, with no sign and no leading zero. Any other name under `claude/` is hand-made, and what the library makes of it is G1's question.
- **The holder of claude/\<n\>:** the worker whose push created the branch, as the step that made it records. That push's claim file names the same worker on its `- Worker:` line (line 31).
- **What the readers show** for a branch: its row on the board, issue and worker (board.claim_rows, board.py:526-536); the numbers board.claimed_numbers (323-326) and team.claim_branches (team.py:144-147) take it to claim; and, for a pull request from it, protocol_check.claim_number (protocol_check.py:33-36).
- **Idle at t:** more than 24 hours between the branch's last commit and t, the last commit being when GitHub received the push that set the head, as the activity records it, any other entry of the branch dating it too, whatever heads it names, when it came after the run began, and an older one that names another head stale (SPEC["moved off"], since F8's second review and its fix check), read once more after a pause where the first read lists no such push or GitHub gives it no answer that holds, and the head's committer date where the second read lists none either or GitHub refuses the read for good, but the newest entry either read listed where that is later (the spec's Release, since F8's fix and its reviews; SPEC["last commit"], SPEC["undated push"], SPEC["floor"]). Until that fix it was the committer date alone (line 67: "by the committer date of its head"), which whoever pushes sets (F8).
- **Finished:** the issue is labelled in-review, or a pull request from the branch is marked ready, or a draft's author's latest comment on it starts with "finished; ready to mark" (line 67).
- **Release comment:** a comment on the issue whose first line is `<!-- sdlc-coordinator: claim-released -->` (line 67), by the repository's owner, a member of its organization, a collaborator, `github-actions[bot]`, or anyone else who may write to the repository (line 69).
- **due(n) at t:** claude/\<n\> exists, the issue is open, the claim is not finished, it is idle at t, and no release comment came more than 24 hours after the branch's last commit (line 67: the coordinator "skips a claim whose latest release comment came more than 24 hours after the branch's last commit"); and, for a head a release already kept, released(n) is false: no release comment counts, or the label claimed was put back (line 69, SPEC["kept deleted"]: the coordinator "releases it only where the board counts no release of it", since F8's fix and its review), so a kept head is not released twice for the same work.
- **released(n):** line 69's sentence. The issue is open, labelled neither claimed nor in-review; its pull request, if any, is still a draft; and its latest release comment came more than 24 hours after the branch's last commit, with no commit since, or, since F8's fix, the head is one a release already kept, a branch `released/<n>-<sha7>` standing at it, for which any release comment that counts will do (SPEC["pushed again"]). The library's own reading also excepts a finished draft (F10); the oracle follows the spec.
- **A piece of open work:** a claim, or an open pull request; a claim and the pull requests from its branch are one piece, named by its issue in the overlap markers and told on that issue, a marker counting for the claim only from the push that made its branch (since F2's fix the spec says so). Line 73 has the coordinator tell "each piece of open work about the work it meets, once" (F2).

### 2.3 The guarantees

Each guarantee is one function in validation/invariants.py, run at one of five kinds of moment:
- **state:** after every step;
- **cycle:** on every board run, given the state before and after it and the run's write calls;
- **command:** on every command a worker runs;
- **gate:** on every step of the gate's world;
- **dealing:** on every set of answers the clerk gives the workers of one run.

The spec column gives line numbers in spec/PROTOCOL.md. A guarantee that names effects requires them to happen when they are owed, not only at most once: a coordinator that posted nothing passed the first design's "at most once" versions of G7 and G10.

| Id | Guarantee | Spec | Code under test | Kind |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G1 | **One name, one holder, by what every reader shows.** For every open issue n, the branches that any reader takes to name n are at most one, and it is claude/\<n\>. From the push that creates claude/\<n\> until a release or a merge frees the name, the board names its holder and no other worker while the claim file is on the branch; once the finishing push has deleted the file, it names the worker the issue's label gives, else says there is no claim file, the reading board.claim_row documents. Defining G1 through `claims.number_of` alone would let it pass vacuously if that function grew too strict, and a plain push that edits the claim file's Worker line changes whom the board names without changing who pushed first. | 3, 29, 104 | claims.key_of, number_of and claim_worker (claims.py:78-115); board.claim_rows (526-536); team.claim_branches (144-147); protocol_check.claim_number (33-36) | state |
| G2 | **A release comment only on a due claim.** Every release comment the coordinator posts is on an issue that was due when the run began. | 67 | coordinator.is_candidate and releases_due (41-63); claims.release_due (194-208) | cycle |
| G3 | **A due claim is released, in the spec's order.** In a run with no injected fault: claimed and every worker label taken off first, and todo put on where the issue carries none of todo, epic and blocker (F15); then one release comment by `github-actions[bot]`, the marker on its first line, and no release comment posted while a label it takes off is still on (the order of 0.4.1; cf3bdb0 posted the comment first, F1); then claude/\<n\> renamed to `released/<n>-<first seven of its head>`; the branch's open pull request closed by the rename; and n in the Queue. A difference the cycle check finds in such an issue's labels is G8's (invariants.freeing_guarantee, freeing_order). | 17, 67 | labels.holder_names and reserved (67-118); coordinator.labels_released (137-143); board.given_rows (565-580); since F22's fix coordinator.reopened_due and freeing, github.free_reopened | state; for F22, cycle |

(5) It merges only a branch that merges cleanly with main as main is now. (6) MainMoved leaves main as it was. (7) Main only moves forward. Each worker carries its own claims, the ones whose claim files name it, whatever the issue's labels say, and no other's; one with none takes from the first of Pick's places that has anything for it, a task given to it alone before one the labels of several workers give, which goes to the first of them in the team's order with nothing else to do, and takes nothing only where Pick leaves it nothing or lets it wait (F18). | 17, 25, 77 | team.deal, deals, moves_from and deal_moves; team.Snapshot.holder, carry, given, own_move and pool; next_for is one worker's answer from the same rule | dealing |

A read the run does without, of how a held branch stands (F9) or of when a branch began (F13), refused, fails no run either.
