The run's log says which, one line for each such branch (github.pushed_refused_lines, as F13's undated branches are): `<branch>: <why>, so its idleness is read from its newest entry's time, <time>, later than its committer date` (ending `GitHub not showing its commit` where the commit's read answered nothing), `<branch>: <why>, so its idleness is read from its committer date`, or, in the run its push started, `<branch>: <why>, so its idleness is read from the start of this run, <time>, which its push started`, the why being the last read's, else the first's where the first listed an entry, either that the repository's activity lists no push that set the branch's head (github.NO_PUSH_LISTED; until F8's third review it said "read twice", which held for neither a first read GitHub refused nor a second it answered with a 404) or that GitHub refused to say, with its answer in parentheses. A 404, a repository GitHub keeps no activity of, lists no entry, so it has no floor and is dated by its committer dates as it always was, with no line, outside the run its push started, which dates it at its start with no line either; a 404 on the second read after a first that listed an entry keeps the first's floor and why, and after a first that listed none of the branch's, or that GitHub refused, is what a 404 always was, no floor and no line (F8's third review). board.Release carries pushed_at beside committed_at and reads its last commit from the first it has (Release.last_commit), so the board, the coordinator, the release's text and the clerk (github.claim_released) read one time. And a branch pushed again at a head a release already kept, which the board deletes since its work is kept (S07), is released by that release whenever it came, before that push or after (board.release_counts, Release.kept, read from the branch list the run already holds), since dated by its push alone it would stand a day as a claim nobody holds; the coordinator releases such a branch only where the board counts no release of it, no release comment counting or the label claimed put back, once it has gone a day without a commit (coordinator.kept_released, through board.is_released since F8's review: as first built it read the release comment alone, so a release the label claimed undid held such a claim for good). Where GitHub answers the post of that release with an error, the release comment the run then finds is the one GitHub wrote and lost its answer to only when it is newer than the latest the run held (github.landed_release, github.newer, since F8's second review): for a kept head an earlier release counts whenever it came, so it passed for this run's, and the labels stayed off with no comment of the run's. The spec's Release section says all of it, any entry after the run began and the stale one in the clause SPEC["moved off"] quotes and the floor in the clause SPEC["floor"] quotes.
- **What happened.** A release read the head's committer date (spec line 67; github.committed_at, adapters/github.py:353-357 at cf3bdb0), which the person pushing sets. A commit dated in the future held a claim past its idle day; a commit dated in the past was released at once. The canary uses the second on purpose (C6). On a copy with the fix that backdated push in C6 and C7 is F8's own live check, its claim not released at once (known while the canary vendors 0.4.1), and the release by idleness they follow is that of a claim an earlier run held in review at least 25 hours before (section 11).
- **The critique's view.** The first design asked whether the spec should say that idleness is measured by committer date; it already did (line 67: "by the committer date of its head"), so that question was moot. The real choice was whether to read the server's `timestamp` of the push from the activity API instead (canary C5), which the fix does.
- **The fix check.** A check of the second review's fix (d952605, e34de7d in the harness) found three gaps, which bd00f9d closes in the library and the spec, and which the harness now shows. (a) Where both reads lagged, the newest entry listed was a stale one, the committer date stood in, and a commit dated back had its claim released in the run its own push started, which 286ba37, the build before that review, held where the stale entry moved the branch off the head, a head pushed back, and no build before the floor held where it named the head on neither side (its third review narrowing the claim). The floor closes it: on F8's trace c's commit at hour 129, dated 48 hours back, read a push behind twice (pushes-lagging-twice), is dated by its push of 128 and held at step 41, where "the floor dropped" releases it (G2, G3, G10). (b) After two pushes since the run read the branch, the newest entry names the head the run read on neither side, and it dated nothing. Any entry after the run began dates the claim now: at step 44 (pushes-moved-twice, two pushes after the run read the branch on the first read) the run reads once, where "a fresh entry dating only off the head" reads again and logs a floor no read owes (G2). (c) No lane caught a library that drops now on the second read, since a fault that lists a push after the run read the branch (pushes-moved) answers the first read too, which dates it, and a second read after a lagging one lists the push that set the head, which dates it without now. pushes-500-then-moved, a 500 and then that push, is that lane, at step 43, where "the second read judged with no moment of the run" dates nothing by the entry, takes it for the floor at the same minute and says so in its log, a line no read owes (G2). Two more mutants hold the floor to the spec's clause: at step 42 (pushes-lagging-then-500, a read a push behind and then a 500) "the floor of the last read alone" releases the claim (G2, G3, G10), and at step 39 (pushes-lagging-twice with the floor at the commit's own minute, c's commit at 128 dated an hour back) "a floor at the committer date taken for later" says the claim was read from its floor (G2). That mutant, as (c)'s, dates the claim at the same minute as the library, so it shows in no write and no read count: only the run's log tells it apart, which is why the harness now judges the log (invariants.pushes_logged). A third review of that check (F8's third review; the trace's steps 45 to 51) found three gaps in the harness: no fault had the two reads list different stale entries, so "the floor of the first read alone" and "the earlier floor" slipped, which pushes-catching-up, two pushes behind and then one, now catches at step 45 (G3); no fault listed a delete after the run read the branch, so "a fresh delete dates nothing" slipped, which pushes-deleted catches at step 47 (G2); and a link of a chain fired on the first call it matched, before its head was spent, which held only while both matched the same calls (FakeGitHub.fault_for). And it found the first-push gap: a claim whose activity lags both reads with no entry that dates it, a fresh branch with none at all or a claim made again whose newest entry is the board's delete of the name, had its claim commit dated back released in the run its own push started, the floor or the committer date deciding; the spec now dates such a head at the run's start in that run (SPEC["started by"]), which "the run's event ignored" undoes at step 49 (G3).
- **Evidence.** Canary C5 on symphony-protocol-canary, run by hand on 2026-10-15 (Actions run 38767876865): after a lease push onto a throwaway claim, the activity listed the `force_push` with its `actor`, `timestamp`, `before` and `after`, 3 of 3 expectations held. validation/traces/F8.json: a's claim dated 72 hours ahead, b's commit at hour 24 dated 48 hours back and c's claim. At hour 25 the run's read of a's push lists a push after the run read the branch (the fault pushes-moved), so a's claim, dated by that push, is held, b's, pushed an hour before, is held and c's is released; the next run releases a's, a day idle by its push. At hour 26 the run whose first read of b's push GitHub answers with a 500 (pushes-500), and the one whose first read is answered before the activity lists the push (pushes-lagging), each read once more, are answered and hold b's claim; the run GitHub refuses every read of b's push for good (pushes-403) reads it once, dates it by its committer date and releases it. c, then a and b, push their released heads again, a and b putting the label claimed back; the run at hour 27 deletes `claude/3`, whose release at 25 counts for it though it came before the push at 26, and leaves `claude/1` and `claude/2` as claims; the owner takes claimed off b's issue at 50, and the run at 51 releases a's branch again, a day without a push and no release the board counts, and deletes both, with no second release of b's. At 76, a day after that delete, a pushes its kept head again and puts claimed back, and the run whose first read is a push behind (pushes-lagging) finds the board's delete of 51 newest, a stale entry off the head, reads once more and holds the claim; at 101 the run that releases it, a day without a push, meets a refused comment (comment-500) and puts claimed back, and the next releases it whole; c claims #3 anew, commits at 102 and at 127 pushes claude/3 back to its claim commit with a lease (the verb drop-commits, which no search offers), and the run whose first read is a push behind finds the commit's push of 102 newest, stale, reads once more and holds c's claim. Since F8's fix check, c commits at 128, dated an hour back to the minute of that forced push, and the run whose reads are both a push behind (pushes-lagging-twice) finds that push newest, stale, its time no later than the commit's date, so the committer date stands in, holds the claim and says so in its log; then c commits at 129, dated 48 hours back, and four runs hold its claim: one whose reads are both a push behind, by the floor, c's push of 128, later than the commit's date of 81; one whose first read is a push behind and whose second GitHub refuses with a 500 (pushes-lagging-then-500), by the first read's floor; one whose first read GitHub refuses with a 500 and whose second lists a push after the run read the branch (pushes-500-then-moved), by that push; and one whose first read lists two pushes after the run read the branch (pushes-moved-twice), by the newer, which names c's head on neither side, read once. Since F8's third review three more runs at 129 hold it: one whose first read is two pushes behind, the branch's making all it lists, and whose second is a push behind (pushes-catching-up), by the later floor, c's push of 128; one whose first read GitHub refuses with a 500 and whose second is a push behind (pushes-500-then-lagging), by that read's floor; and one whose read lists the branch's delete after the run read it (pushes-deleted), by that delete, read once. Then a claims #1 anew at 130, its claim commit dated 48 hours back, and the run that push started, the event create on claude/1, whose reads are both a push behind and list the board's delete of 101 newest, holds it, dated at the run's start, where by that delete's time, 29 hours back, it would be released; c commits at 131, and the run at 155 releases a's claim, a day without a push, and holds c's. Since F8's follow-up's second review c drops its commits at 181, two days after its last push, and pushes claude/3 back to its claim commit, the head the branch's making at 101 set, and the run that push started, the event create standing in for the synchronize of c's pull request, which the model does not play, reads the activity two pushes behind, listing that making of 101 newest, 80 hours back, since the model's activity keeps a branch's making and its last two pushes: it dates the head at its start and holds the claim, its log saying the newest push the activity lists of the head came before the run began, and the next run, whose read lists the forced push of 181, holds it by that push. The review of that change found the trace's first build saying that read listed a forced push of 156, which the model never lists there, and its two steps at 156 and 157 changing nothing, so they are gone; it found oracle.own_push_dates pinned by that one entry alone, which tests/test_validation_oracle.py and tests/test_validation_invariants.py now pin at the slack's edge and in a run's log. Green since the fix, and red under nineteen mutants, which the lane credits to the regression traces (validation/mutants.py, 114 in all): at step 13 for G2 and step 14 for G3 under "idleness by the committer date" (github.head_since reading the committer date alone), which releases b's claim and holds a's; at step 13 for G2 under "the before side ignored" (github.pushed_by dating an entry by its `after` alone), which reads the activity again for a's claim, and at steps 43 and 44 since the fix check; at step 15 for G2 under "the push read once", which reads b's push once under the 500; at step 23 for G5 under "a kept head dated by its push" (board.release_counts by time alone) and "kept counts only at or after the push", which keep `claude/3` as a claim; and at step 25 for G3 under "a kept release undone holds for good" (coordinator.kept_released as first built), which leaves a's claim unreleased, and for G2 under "a kept head released again" (no head read as kept), which releases b's twice; and, since F8's second review, at steps 28 and 37 for G2 under "a stale entry off the head dates it" (github.pushed_by dating any entry that names the head on either side, whatever its age, as at 286ba37), which reads once and releases a's kept head and c's claim in the runs their pushes started, and at step 29 for G3L under "a kept head's old release taken for the lost comment" (landed_release with no newer), which leaves a's labels off; and, since F8's fix check, at step 39 for G2 under "a floor at the committer date taken for later" (head_since dating by a floor no earlier than the commit's date), at step 41 for G2, G3 and G10 under "the floor dropped" (head_since with no floor), at step 42 for the same under "the floor of the last read alone" (head_since with the last read's floor), at step 43 for G2 under "the second read judged with no moment of the run" (head_since reading again with no now), and at step 44 for G2 under "a fresh entry dating only off the head" (github.pushed_by dating a fresh entry only where its `before` is the head), each as the fix check above says; and, since F8's third review, at step 45 for G3 under "the floor of the first read alone" (head_since with the first read's floor) and "the earlier floor" (head_since with the earlier of the two reads' floors), which release c's claim in the run whose first read is two pushes behind, at step 47 for G2 under "a fresh delete dates nothing" (github.pushed_by dating no delete after the run began), which reads again and logs a floor no read owes, and at step 49 for G3 under "the run's event ignored" (head_since with no started_by), which releases a's claim anew in the run its own push started; and, since F8's follow-up's second review, at step 53 for G3, G2 and G10 under "an older push trusted in the run its push started" (head_since dating by such a push in that run, as the follow-up first built it), which releases c's claim in the run its own push started, as the review found by playing that run under pushes-catching-up, the oracle then agreeing with the library, so no lane caught it. head_since's mutants run a copy of it in validation/mutants.py with six rules as arguments, the second read, its now, the floor, the floor's strictness, the run's event and an older push in that run, and tests/test_validation_mutants.py holds each line of the copy to one of the library's but the six that take those rules, and the copy, with every argument at its default, to F8's trace played green. Two more read a release in the clerk as the board did before the fix, by the committer date and with no head kept (github.claim_released), and the clerk's deal against Pick catches them on its claim states released-ahead and kept (dealing.CLAIM_STATES, section 9.1), where the claim the board counts released is dealt as held. Under a mutant that keeps c's release from happening the push names a released branch that does not stand; such a step is not played and is a mismatch of the record (run.Unresolved), so the lane, which reads violations, plays the trace under every mutant. The model records each push that moves a head, with the head it moved the branch from, and the head each branch's making set (model.pushed, model.made_heads; the git world's push the same), with, since F8's second review, the entry before the last push, the push before it or, for a claim branch a push made again, the delete that took it away (model.earlier, model.deleted), and the fake's activity lists the making, that entry and the last push with both sides, a delete as a branch_deletion naming the head it took away as its before; two faults refuse the read of a claim's push, pushes-500 once and pushes-403 for good, and three answer it in place of the model (Fault.answer): pushes-lagging and pushes-lagging-twice with the activity before the last push, a push behind, so the entry before it is newest, the first read or the first two, and pushes-moved with a push after the run read the branch, at the run's minute, which answers every later read of that branch in the run, as GitHub would list it still; since F8's fix check pushes-moved-twice lists a second push after that one, whose entry names the head the run read on neither side, and a fault may name the fault that answers once it is spent (Fault.then), so pushes-500-then-moved refuses the first read with a 500 and lists a push after the run read the branch on the second, and pushes-lagging-then-500 answers the first a push behind and refuses the second with a 500; since F8's third review pushes-catching-up answers the first read two pushes behind and the second one behind, so the two reads list different stale entries, pushes-500-then-lagging refuses the first read with a 500 and answers the second a push behind, pushes-deleted lists the branch's delete after the run read it, at the run's minute, as pushes-moved lists a push, and a link of a chain fires only once the link before it is spent (FakeGitHub.fault_for), where as the fix check built it a link fired on the first call it matched. The oracle reads a branch's last commit from the model (oracle.head_time), and a run is judged against the model as it read each push, from what its reads of that push were answered with (invariants.push_readings, invariants.push_dates, oracle.dated): a branch whose last read dates its push by that push, one whose read lists a push after the run read it by that push, unless a push to that branch started the run and the entry came more than MOVED_SLACK_MINUTES before the run began (oracle.own_push_dates, since F8's follow-up's second review), and one that no read dates, or that one so, at the start of the run that a push to the branch started (oracle.pushed_branch, STARTED_BY_EVENTS, the push and create events, which the harness's runs name; SPEC["started by"]), else by the newest entry either read listed where that entry is strictly later than its committer date (oracle.floor_dates, SPEC["floor"]), else by its committer date; each entry is read from the spec's words (oracle.entry_dates: one that set the head dates it, and any other, whatever heads it names, only when it came after the run began, five minutes before allowed as the library allows, the model's clock at the run's start being the now the run is handed, an older one stale and owed a second read). invariants.pushes_read_again holds the run to reading a push twice exactly where its first read may tell more (G2), which no comparison of writes shows, since a run that reads once agrees with the oracle's view of what it read; and, since F8's fix check, invariants.pushes_logged holds the run's log to one line for each branch whose reads owe one, a branch that no read dated by a push and whose reads give a why, saying it was read from its floor at that entry's time, from its committer date or from the start of the run its push started, and to none for a branch its reads dated or one GitHub keeps no activity of (G2), the words those of the three clauses oracle.LOG_WORDS spells, which tests/test_validation_guarantees.py holds to a run's own log, since a floor at the committer date, or a fresh entry a second read missed and took for the floor, dates the claim at the same minute and shows in no write. activity-500 refuses only the reads of a branch's making, so F13's traces keep their meaning. Tests: tests/test_github.py (the push dating the head, the entries that date nothing, a refusal falling back and logged, a kept head, the clerk's calls), tests/test_board.py (the last commit, a kept head, the round trip), tests/test_coordinator.py (due by the push, a kept head released once), tests/test_cli.py (the log line), and the harness's model, fake, oracle and invariants tests, with, since F8's fix check, the floor's reads and the log's words (tests/test_validation_invariants.py, tests/test_validation_guarantees.py) and the copy of head_since (tests/test_validation_mutants.py), and, since F8's third review, the run's event (tests/test_cli.py, tests/test_validation_cycle.py, tests/test_validation_invariants.py, tests/test_validation_guarantees.py) and the fake's faults and chains (tests/test_validation_fake_github.py).
- **The cost.** A board run pays one call for a claim where the activity dates the push at once, as before the fix, the one call that read the commit now reading the activity; up to three where it does not, the read, a pause and one more, and the commit where the second dates none either, two in the run the branch's push started where neither read dates it, since the commit is not read then; and, where GitHub refuses the read for good, as for a token that may not read the activity, two calls for every candidate on every run, the refused read and the commit, where it paid one before the fix. The floor costs no call, its entry being the one each read already fetched, nor does dating by any entry after the run began. The clerk's claim_released asks one call more than before, for the released branch that would keep the head, which the board reads from the branch list it already has, beside what head_since costs.
- **What it leaves.** A push the activity has not listed by the second read, a pause after the first, dates nothing outside the run it started, so the later of the newest entry it does list and the committer date stands in for that run, which holds the claim at least a day from that entry, and the committer date alone where neither read lists an entry of the branch, as where GitHub refuses the first read for good; in the run the push started the head is dated at the run's start unless a read lists an entry from the last five minutes before the run began or after it, which dates it, so what is left of the first-push gap is a run another event started while the activity still lags behind the branch's push, which dates the head as before, by the floor or the committer date: in the template the Announce's two labels and the draft's opening each start a run within seconds of the claim's push, queued behind the create run, so where the activity lags that long a claim commit dated back is released one run later than before the fix, a window no canary measures yet, which a canary check recording when the activity first lists a push would size; a pull_request synchronize GitHub fires for a change of the base, with no push, dates the claim at that run's start and holds it one run, and so does a re-run, which keeps the event of the run it repeats, its GITHUB_REF and payload alike, however long after the push it comes; a run that began more than five minutes after the push that started it, queued behind others in the template's one concurrency group for the board, reads that push, which the activity lists, as an older one and dates the head at its start, a little after the push, which holds the claim no longer than the queue held the run, its log saying only that the newest push listed came before the run began; GitHub runs no pull_request workflow while a pull request has a merge conflict, so a push to a conflicted claim's pull request starts no run, and the next run, which another event starts, dates that push by the activity as any run does; and the harness plays no pull_request synchronize, its runs naming the push and create events, where the template's board.yml reaches a claim's later pushes through synchronize alone (cli.pushed_branch, tests/test_cli.py); that a lagging read lists the activity as it stood before the push, as the fake's does, no canary check reads; that GitHub lists a branch's delete as an entry naming the head it took away, canary C6 now records after the delete of the freed name's second push, and the owner's next run of it settles. A branch renamed back by "putting the label claimed back" keeps the push record of its released name in the model; whether GitHub's activity lists a rename as a new entry under the name it makes, which on a rename back would hold the claim a day from the rename, canary C6 now records, reading both names' newest entries once the board's rename is seen (section 11, What C6 records of the activity), and the owner's next run of it settles, a rename back being GitHub's same rename with the names swapped. A claim whose release the label claimed undid is skipped by the coordinator, its head kept or not, while its latest release comment came more than a day after its branch's last commit, as the spec's candidates sentence says; one whose release came sooner, as a kept head's before the push that set it again, is released again once it has gone a day without a commit. And the activity's retention, which GitHub's description does not state, bounds how old a push it can date; past it the committer date stands in.

**F9. A draft may never be told "main moved".** Status: fixed after 0.4.1 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14: a draft hears "main moved" like any other pull request): github.standing_of reads a pull request whose mergeable_state is one of the opaque words, blocked or draft (OPAQUE_STATES), through its mergeable flag and one compare (github.behind_by, now a function of the module, which GitHubWorld.behind_by calls too) into a standing in the board's words, and pull_with_detail writes it beside the detail; board.Pull gained the field standing, read by from_dict and pr_state and written by to_dict only when set, which Pull.state reads before the word: behind and conflicting win, a draft whose branch is clean reads clean rather than unknown, and a blocked branch that is clean stays blocked, which says what still stops it; so pulls_behind_main speaks to a draft or a blocked pull request once the adapter read its standing, and a snapshot with no standing, the fixtures' and the memory world's with opaque_pulls off, renders as before. standing_of leaves a pull request stacked on a branch other than the default branch the caller names to GitHub's word, since "main moved" speaks of the default branch, and reads a comparison GitHub refuses, a 5xx, a 422 or a rate limit's 403, as unknown, which keeps GitHub's word, as the coordinator's own comparison does (github.holding), so one pull request GitHub will not compare fails no run. GitHub.open_pull carries the same standing to the clerk for a pull request marked ready, whose claim_needs_work deals a claim back for a branch behind its base, as the board tells one behind the default branch "main moved" (team.STANDING_NEEDS_REBASE), and none for a draft, which the clerk carries whatever its standing; GitHubWorld.standing reads the pull request without one (GitHub.pull_detail) and compares the branch itself through standing_of, so a comparison GitHub refuses is unknown there too, and the gate's merge reads its pull request without a comparison. The fake answers "blocked" for every pull request not in conflict when the model's opaque_pulls is on (validation/fake_github.py, model.py, whose state key holds the switch, scenarios.py, replay.py and run.py carry it), F9's trace sets it and expects the comment, the oracle reads the spec with no switch for F9 (oracle.main_moved_effects), known.json's fixed_after names F9 at 0.4.1, and two mutants undo the fix (validation/mutants.py, 34 in all): "an opaque state read by GitHub's word alone" puts 0.4.1's reading back and is caught on the trace at step 4, and "the comparison read by ahead_by" takes the comparison's other count and is caught at step 2, both for G10.
- **What happened.** board.state_word maps GitHub's mergeable_state "draft" to unknown and reads "blocked" as a word of its own (board.py:293-302 at cf3bdb0), which up to 0.4.1 was the whole reading and since the fix is the reading of a pull request with no standing; tests/test_board.py pins that mapping (line 333 at cf3bdb0), and tests/fixtures/board-wide.json:21 uses it. The "main moved" rule speaks only to pull requests behind or conflicting (board.py:417-420 at cf3bdb0), so a pull request under either word never heard it, and neither word says how the branch stands to main. GitHub answers "blocked" while a rule holds the pull request: on every copy's default branch while its required check is pending or red, and in crowd mode while it waits for its approval. So 0.4.1 told no pull request behind main whose check had not passed, a draft whose check was running or red among them, and no unapproved pull request behind main in crowd mode; one in conflict reads "dirty" whatever its check, and was told; one whose check passed reads "behind" under the template's main ruleset, which requires an up-to-date branch, and was told, which is what canary C2 waits for before it judges the word. The gate's world, by contrast, read such a pull request through mergeable and behind_by (adapters/github.py:657-670 at cf3bdb0), which is the reading the fix gives the board.
- **Evidence.** C0, read by hand on 2026-10-14 on two live drafts of this repository: GitHub's REST API answered mergeable_state "clean" for a clean draft (#23 while it was one) and "dirty" for a conflicting one (#21), never "draft", so a draft's word is its branch's standing as any pull request's, and "blocked" comes from a rule that holds it. validation/traces/F9.json, a draft level with main and then behind it under opaque_pulls, with a board run each time, is green as built, the comment told only once main moved, and red for G10 under each mutant: at step 2 under the one that reads ahead_by and at step 4 under the one that takes GitHub's word alone; a draft one commit ahead and one behind, the trace's only state before the review of 2026-10-14, reads the same under either count. The search "a draft GitHub calls blocked" (validation/mutants.py), from a claim and its draft with opaque_pulls on to depth 3, finds nothing on the library and catches both mutants, which tests/test_validation_mutants.py holds, since the lane credits both to the traces. tests/test_github.py holds standing_of, pull_with_detail and open_pull (an opaque state resolved through one compare into a standing; a comparison GitHub cannot make left unknown; one GitHub refuses, a 403, 422, 500, 502 or 503, read as unknown with GitHub's word kept; mergeable false read as conflicting with no compare; a word the board reads as it is given no standing and no compare; a pull request stacked on a branch other than the default branch left to GitHub's word, which collect names; the standing open_pull carries for a pull request marked ready and not for a draft; the gate's world comparing the branch itself, once, a refused comparison read as unknown whether GitHub computed the mergeability at the first read or after the pause, and the merge paying for no comparison), tests/test_team.py a ready claim that a check or a review holds carried once its branch is behind, tests/test_parity.py the standing as a difference the template never read, and tests/test_board.py holds Pull.standing (a blocked pull request takes the standing the rule speaks to and a clean one stays blocked; a draft's word with a standing reads it; one with no standing reads as before; "main moved" reaches a blocked one once its standing is read; the standing written only when set and read back, by pr_state too).
- **The cost.** One compare in every board run for each open pull request GitHub calls blocked or draft, and, for each such pull request behind main, the read of its comments that finds the marker: with 30 such pull requests, all behind and already told, a run takes 154 calls where 0.4.1 took 94 (the review of 2026-10-14, counted over the fake). Nothing is cached between runs, since a run reads the repository as it is. Beside the board, the clerk pays one compare each time it asks whether a claim whose pull request is marked ready and called blocked needs work (each deal, and each further round of a worker in Actions), none for a draft, which it carries whatever its standing; the gate's world one when it reads a standing, and none to merge. And a ready claim whose check still runs is carried at once when its branch is behind, where 0.4.1 waited for the check and, under a rule that asks for an up-to-date branch, for GitHub to say behind: a busy default branch can take a worker in Actions round after round, each push starting the check again, up to its limit of rounds (team.claim_needs_work, and team.integration_due for the integration's claim).
- **Not covered by this fix.** GitHub calls a branch that is behind "behind" only under a rule that requires it to be up to date before merging, as the template's main and organization rulesets do; under one that does not, as its crowd ruleset (docs/setup/ruleset-crowd.json, strict false), or with no rule at all, a pull request whose rules are met reads "clean" while behind (scripts/canary.py, C2's note), or "unstable", GitHub's word for one that may merge while a check that is not required fails, and this fix took those words as they are, so such a pull request never hears "main moved". That is F16, settled since by the spec's words: such a pull request may merge as it stands and is owed nothing.
- **The critique's view.** One REST GET on any open draft settles what GitHub answers, with no canary repository (C0); GraphQL marks `MergeStateStatus.DRAFT` deprecated (github/docs src/graphql/data/fpt/schema.docs.graphql:26531-26536), so the REST answer was not known until the GET was made. Then: should drafts hear "main moved", read the way GitHubWorld reads them? The owner said yes, and the fix reads them so.

**F10. Releasing a finished draft by hand never takes effect.** Status: fixed in 0.4.1 (#11).
- **What happens.** github.releases skips a finished draft before it reads any release comment (adapters/github.py:394), so board.is_released never sees the person's release. The spec counts it as released: line 69 excepts only in-review and a pull request marked ready, and line 67 says a person "may release by the same steps". The labels come off, but the board keeps showing the claim held, and the issue stays out of the Queue.
- **Evidence.** `python3 validation/prototype/probe_finished_by_hand.py`: the oracle says released, with the Queue `[1]`; the board shows `claude/1` held, and still does thirty days later. `python3 validation/prototype/find_t3.py` finds the trace: claim, wait, announce, draft, finished, release by hand, board.
- **The fix's choice.** Either the release takes effect, or a by-hand release of a finished draft is refused as a whole rather than half applied.

**F11. One refused comment fails the whole board run, and the board branch is not written.** Status: fixed in 0.4.1 (#11).
- **What happens.** post_comments and comment_once (adapters/github.py:436-452) let GitHubError escape, while coordinate (463-465) and release_claim (548-554) deliberately swallow theirs. board_live_run has already written STATE.md locally (cli.py:110) and then raises at 116, and run_board exits 1 (121-127). symphony-template's step that writes the branch board (board.yml:132-134) runs only after success, so it is skipped.
- **Evidence.** `python3 validation/prototype/probe_comment_failure.py` prints `board: POST /repos/o/r/issues/100/comments: HTTP 500` and exit code 1, with STATE.md written locally. With a refused comment POST in scope, 116 board runs raised at depth 6, before the reductions (`python3 validation/prototype/ext_verbs.py`).
- **Effect.** A secondary rate limit does the same. A comment GitHub refuses for good would freeze the board branch: the marker is never written, so every run tries the comment again and fails again.
- **The fix, and the harness.** Catch the error per comment, as release_claim does; the harness adds G15 and faults on every write endpoint.

**F12. "Main moved" is lost under the board's concurrency group.** Status: fixed after 0.4.1 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14), both ways: board.comments_due tells every pull request behind or conflicting with main at its current commit on any run, once per commit by the marker's sha, so a replaced run costs nothing but a delay to the next run, which is at most the schedule's six hours; and symphony-template's board.yml adds `queue: max` to the concurrency group, which github.com offers, so the runs a merge fires wait in turn instead of replacing one another. The mutant that keys the word on the push's run again turns F12's trace and S05q red for G10. A refused "main moved" comment is now due again on the next run, as an idle one was already. The canary's C2 judges the word by the state too, the marker on the pull request whichever run posts it, and reads it as known while symphony-protocol-canary vendors 0.4.1 (known.json's `fixed_after`, section 11), new from the first release after.
- **What happened.** "Main moved" was the only rule keyed on the event that started the run (board.py:469 of 0.4.1). symphony-template's board.yml:83-85 set `group: board` with no `queue`, and by default a newer run replaces the one pending in the group (github/docs data/reusables/actions/actions-group-concurrency.md).
- **Why it is common.** A merge fires pull_request closed, a push to main, issues closed and delete at nearly the same moment, and the push trigger at board.yml:62 has no branch filter, so every worker's push to a `claude/` branch also queues a run, whose job board.yml:94 then skips; that such a run still takes the pending slot is inferred, not measured. So the push to main's run is often replaced, and that commit's "main moved" comments are never posted.
- **The fix's options.** Make "main moved" depend on state: on any run, tell each behind or conflicting pull request that has no marker for main's current sha. Or add `queue: max` to the group, which github.com offers (github/docs data/features/actions-nga.yml). The model has the transition "a pending run is replaced" (section 3.3), and canary C2 measures the drops.

**F13. The landing order's "oldest" is the lowest number.** Status: fixed after 0.4.1 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14: the library fix on every item): the oldest is the work that began first. github.began_times reads, for the open work in the Overlaps alone, the newest push that made each branch from the repository's activity, a time GitHub writes as the push reaches it, so no clock of a worker's own comes in, as the committer date would (F8): one call lists the repository's newest hundred branch creations (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/activity` filtered to `branch_creation`, newest first, github.newest_creations), and each branch it does not name is asked about on its own, filtered to that branch (github.branch_made_at), so a run pays one call where the work is new, as it is while it overlaps, and a refused first call costs only the reads it would have saved. collect writes the times by branch as the snapshot's `began`, with GitHub's answer for each branch it refused to date as `began_refused`, which the run's log names with what the Overlaps then do, a pull request's work dated by its opening and a claim's put last (github.began_refused_lines), and board.Pull gained `created_at`, when the pull request was opened, read as text whatever a snapshot written by hand holds, since the coordinator keys the work it tells by the work itself, and `fork`, whether its branch stands in another repository, read from the API's head and base, and from the key `fork` where a shape written by hand names neither by its full name (board.from_fork). Two pull requests from one head, as to the default branch and to a release branch, ask about their branch once. board.open_work gives each piece its branch and when it began: the time of its branch, so a claim and the pull request later opened from it are one piece that began at the claim, else, for a pull request from a branch the snapshot says nothing of, or nothing that reads as a time, or from a fork, whose branch a branch of the same name here is not, its opening (board.pull_began); board.landing_key orders by blocker, then ready, then that time, then the number. Work nothing dates lands last of all (board.UNDATED): only a claim with no pull request yet can be such work, and a claim opens its draft at once, so it is all but always the newest piece; counted as the oldest, as first built, a claim GitHub would not date in the run that tells an overlap was told as landing before work days older, and that comment is not sent again (the fix's review). No such push recorded, no activity at all (a 404), or a branch's own read refused, whatever GitHub answers, leaves the branch undated, and one branch GitHub will not answer for fails no run, as a refused comparison fails none since F9's review (github.standing_of); the harness holds a run to that, since a refused read of the activity or of a comparison no longer excuses a failed run (invariants.READS_DONE_WITHOUT, G15). The spec's Overlaps paragraph says so, the oracle reads it with no switch (SPEC["oldest"]), the fake answers the activity from the model's record of the push that made each branch, newest first unless asked otherwise (validation/fake_github.py), the fault activity-500 refuses a run's first read of it, and activity-500-all every read of it, as for a token that may not read it; the oracle reads the branches whose own read GitHub refused in the run, from the run's calls, as branches nothing dates (invariants.unsaid_branches, oracle.open_work), so the page held to the spec under a refusal is the one the spec asks for then. F13's trace has a's claim on #1 at hour 0, b's on #2 at hour 2, b's draft (#100) at hour 3 and a's (#101) at hour 5, so a's work began first, though its draft opened last and has the higher number; it expects #101, #100, then the same with the first read refused, then, once c claims #3 at hour 6 and every read is refused, #100, #101, #3, each draft dated by its opening and the claim nothing dates last, and is green. known.json's as_built was F2 then, and is empty since F2's fix; its fixed_after names F13 at 0.4.1, and the overlaps property dates its pieces three ways. Five mutants undo the fix (validation/mutants.py, 39 in all): "the landing order by number" puts the old order back and "a pull request dated by its opening" dates each draft by its opening, both caught on the trace at step 4 for G7; "a refused activity read stops the run" lets the refusal escape, caught at step 5 for G15; "a branch never asked about on its own" leaves every branch undated once the first read is refused, caught at step 5 for G7; and "work nothing dates landing first" puts such work first, as first built, caught at step 7 for G7. A pull request from a fork is dated by its opening in the unit tests alone, since the fake opens no pull request from a fork. A pull request from a fork whose branch bears a claim branch's name still stands in for that claim elsewhere on the page and in the coordinator, which is F17. One assumption the fake makes, which canary C6 now records and the owner's next run of it settles: GitHub's activity keeps a renamed branch's creation under its new name, as the model's rename does, read from the newest entries of `released/R-<sha7>` and `claude/R` once the board's rename is seen (section 11, What C6 records of the activity); it matters only to a released claim renamed back, whose order is then its first push's or its rename's, and, since F2's review, which is told again if GitHub dates its rename (F2, What it leaves).
- **What happened.** board.landing_key ordered the open work that meets in a file by blocker, then ready, then the number the work is shown by, where the spec says "then the oldest". A claim with no pull request is shown by its issue's number and one with a pull request by the pull request's, so the two kinds were compared by numbers from two sequences, and a claim made later landed before an older draft whenever its issue's number was the lower.
- **Evidence.** tests/test_validation_invariants.py, `test_f13_the_oldest_work_lands_first`: a claims #1; two hours later b claims #2, which lists the same file, and two hours after that a opens #1's draft (#100); the board's Overlaps row reads #100, #2, as the oracle does, and under either order mutant #2, #100; `test_f13_work_github_will_not_date_lands_as_the_spec_reads_it`: with every read of the activity refused, b's draft dated by its opening lands before a's claim, which nothing dates, and under the mutant that puts such work first after it; and `test_unsaid_branches_are_those_whose_own_read_github_refused`. tests/test_github.py, `WhenWorkBegan`: the one call that lists the newest creations, each branch's first entry its newest; the one call for a branch the list does not name, filtered to its creation; nothing on record, another branch's entry or another kind of activity saying nothing; a refused list asking each branch on its own; a branch's refused read, whatever GitHub answers, read as nothing for that branch alone, with the page still drawn and the log naming it, a pull request's branch as dated by its opening and a claim's as put last; no read where nothing overlaps, none for a fork's pull request, and one for a branch two pull requests share; and the page's order by when the work began. tests/test_board.py: work nothing dates last, a time that is no text read as nothing said, the coordinator's comments drawn whatever a created_at holds, a pull request whose branch time is no time dated by its opening, a fork's pull request dated by its opening whatever a branch here of its name says, and a fork read from shapes written by hand without raising.
- **The choice, made.** Order by when the work began, which the board reads from GitHub's record of the push that made the branch, rather than the claim branch's first commit, whose committer date the pusher sets; or say in the spec that the order is by number. The owner chose the library fix.

**F14. Given to workers needs the label todo.** Status: fixed after 0.4.1 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14): board.given_rows lists every open issue a worker's label names that nobody has claimed, todo or not, epic or not, as the spec's sentence reads, and the page's heading says so; the clerk's own_move takes a given task with or without todo, since the spec has the worker take what Given to workers gives it first, and still never an epic, which the coordinator plans, as the spec's Pick sentence now says. The fix's review found the clerk dealing an issue labelled claimed with no claim branch, as one reopened after its merged branch was deleted, which the board shows in neither section; the clerk now deals such an issue to nobody, as a worker reading the board takes it (team.Snapshot.free; tests/test_team.py, `test_an_issue_labelled_claimed_is_dealt_to_nobody_branch_or_no_branch`). The oracle reads the spec with no switch, F14's trace is green and its known.json entry gone, and the mutant "given to workers needing the label todo" puts the old rule back and is caught on the trace. The template's fixture pins the heading, so its copy goes first (README.md, A change a consumer must answer).
- **What happened.** board.given_rows listed an issue under Given to workers only when it carried todo and not epic (board.py:607-622 of 0.4.1, given_rows), where spec line 17 says "An issue a worker's label names is that worker's alone: it is left out of the board's Queue and shown under Given to workers until the worker claims it", and the coordinator gives a task by "labelling it `worker:<name>`" (line 77, which since F15's fix reads "labelling it todo and `worker:<name>`", as team.open_task does). An open issue labelled worker:a with no todo was shown in neither section.
- **Evidence.** tests/test_validation_invariants.py, `test_f14_an_issue_a_worker_s_label_names_is_shown_under_given_to_workers`: one issue labelled worker:a alone; the page's Given to workers names #1 for a, as the oracle does, and under the mutant it is empty again, for G9.
- **The choice made.** Show every open, unclaimed issue a worker's label names, rather than say in the spec that Given to workers lists only the queue's issues labelled todo.

**F15. A released task that a worker's label alone gave is shown nowhere.** Status: fixed after 0.4.1 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14: the library fix on every finding); found by the review of F14's fix. The coordinator's release puts todo on an issue that carries none of todo, epic and blocker, in the step that takes the labels off and before the release comment (coordinator.labels_put_on, github.put_labels_on), and the snapshot carries it too (coordinator.after_release), so the same run's page lists the issue in the Queue; a release that fails between the labels and the comment takes that todo off again as it puts the labels back, so the issue is as the run found it (github.put_back), and a todo whose putting on was answered with an error is taken off too, since GitHub may have written it, a 404 counting as taken off. An epic gets no todo, since the Queue never lists one, and a blocker none, since the board lists every open blocker under Blockers, claimed or not, the clerk deals one first without todo, and a worker reading the board takes an unclaimed one before the Queue, as the spec's Pick and Blockers paragraphs now say. A task labelled blocked gets todo like any other: the Queue lists it with blocked beside it, and nobody takes it until blocked comes off. The spec's Release paragraph says so, for the coordinator's release and a person's by hand alike, and the oracle reads it with no switch (SPEC["order"], SPEC["put back"]); the invariants tell the release's own todo apart from a label put back (invariants.is_put_back, put_back_refused), fail a todo put on after the release comment (G3), and hold the Blockers section to the oracle, each of its rows and the Queue's with its issue's labels (G9, SPEC["blockers"], invariants.rows_show_labels). F15's trace is green, and eleven mutants undo the fix (validation/mutants.py, 58 in all with the fourth review's eight, below): on the trace, "a release that leaves todo off" at step 7 for G3, "the release's own page without its todo" at step 7 for G5 and "a failed release leaves its todo on" at step 6 for G3L; on the three searches the fix's review added, which seed the issue with labels other than todo, "a refused todo puts no label back" and "a todo written and lost stays on" for G3L, "an epic gets todo" and "a blocker gets todo" for G3; and, from its second review, "the Blockers section left empty" for G9 on the released blocker's search and "a blocked task gets no todo" for G3 on a fourth, a released blocked task; and, from its third review, "the Blockers section without the held blockers" and "a held blocker's row without claimed", both for G9 on a fifth, a held blocker. The fault `label-post-lost` has GitHub write a label and lose its answer. The fast lane searches every fault from a due claim on such a task (tests/test_validation_fast_lane.py).
- **What happened.** An issue a worker's label alone gives, as a person gives one by hand, carries no todo (the coordinator's own tasks carry todo with the worker's label, team.open_task, as the spec's Planning sentence now says); since F14's fix it is shown under Given to workers. Once claimed and released, the release took claimed and the worker's label off (coordinator.labels_released) and nothing went on, so the issue carried neither todo nor a worker's label, and the page showed it in neither the Queue, which lists only an issue labelled todo, nor Given to workers, where the spec says the board lists a released claim's issue again.
- **Evidence.** validation/traces/F15.json: one issue labelled worker:a alone; a claims and announces it; at 25 hours a run whose release comment GitHub refuses puts the labels back and takes the todo off again; the next run releases the claim and the Queue lists #1, labelled todo. tests/test_github.py, `Coordinate`: todo put on between the labels and the comment, none for an issue with todo, an epic or a blocker, taken off again when the comment is refused, and a refused todo putting the labels back and taking off a todo GitHub may have written; tests/test_coordinator.py, labels_put_on and after_release; tests/test_validation_policies.py, `Pick`: a worker takes an unclaimed blocker before the Queue, with or without todo, leaves one held or given to another, never takes an issue labelled blocked or an epic, even one Given to workers gives it, and reads a label worker: with no name after it as no worker's; tests/test_team.py, an issue labelled blocked dealt from neither the Queue nor the Blockers; tests/test_validation_mutants.py, the six searches and their mutants, and every replacement taking its target's arguments, and its required ones alone; tests/test_labels.py and tests/test_coordinator.py, a bare worker: naming nobody, so worker_of passes over it and a release leaves it on.
- **The review.** The fix's review found nothing wrong in the library and four gaps in the harness, all closed here: the invariant skipped its check that the labels were put back after a refused todo, since it counted the 404 to taking off a todo GitHub never wrote as a refusal (invariants.put_back_refused now counts a 404 as taken off, as the library does); every search seeded its issue with todo, so the fix's own paths ran on one trace alone; no fault wrote a label and lost the answer; and three mutants of the fix got past the lane. It also found that a blocker got todo too, listed then under Blockers and in the Queue at once, and that the crowd audit of a copy fails on any todo the board puts on (a counterpart for the template, below).
- **The second review.** The review of those folds found nothing blocking and four gaps, all closed here. The mutant "the labels not put back" still took put_back's three arguments from before the fix, so the lane caught it only by the TypeError its call raised, for G15, and never ran the G3L check it names; it takes the todo too now and is caught for G3L on F15's trace, and a test holds every replacement to its target's arguments (`test_every_replacement_takes_what_its_target_is_called_with`). The lane never read the Blockers section, so a board that listed no blocker passed it; the page and the oracle read it now (page.blockers, oracle.blockers), and the mutant "the Blockers section left empty" is caught. A worker reading the board never took a blocker that carries no todo, a released one or one the template's Blocker form makes, though the clerk deals one first; the spec's Pick now takes an unclaimed blocker before the Queue, its Blockers paragraph says the board lists every open one, and the harness's workers pick so (policies.free_issue). And the todo a released task labelled blocked gets was held by one unit test alone; the search "a released blocked task" holds it in the lane. The comment-first mutant now puts todo on as the release does, so the order is again the one rule it changes. The review also found that crowd mode's rule that only a maintainer queues work would have a way round it if the template's audit accepted every todo a release puts on, which shapes the counterpart below, and stale words: a test's docstring on a released blocked task, the F14 entry's quote of the Planning sentence, section 7's searches, section 3.2's faults, the log's "stay off" for labels whose putting back failed, now "may still be off", and release_one's docstring.
- **The third review.** The review of the second review's folds found nothing blocking; each thing it found is answered here. The spec's Pick gave its reason as "since a blocker stops everyone", beside "that no open blocker stands in the way of" and the Blockers paragraph's "a second worker that sees it waits", which read as no Queue work while any blocker is open, though the clerk deals the Queue beside a held blocker and the harness's workers take from it: Pick now says a blocker comes before other work and takes one "with or without todo" where it names it, and the Blockers paragraph has a worker that finds a blocker held wait for it, help on its pull request or take other work it does not stand in the way of; which work that is, is for the blocker's issue to say, which no row shows, so the harness does not model it (policies.free_issue). The Labels section's todo line said an issue labelled todo and shown unclaimed is free to take, which an issue also labelled blocked is not, and F15 lists such an issue whenever it releases a blocked task; it says so now, and the oracle quotes it (SPEC["todo"]). Pick's "never an epic" now closes its Given to workers clause, where the clerk already reads it (team.own_move), and the harness's workers read the labels of the issues given them to follow it, since that section shows none (policies.View.given); its area clause covers the blockers and the Queue, where the clerk reads one, and the Planning sentence deals a worker's own claim first, then its task, then blockers before the queue, as the clerk does. The lane held only the released half of "claimed or not", and only the rows' numbers: the search "a held blocker" claims the blocker before a board run, and G9 holds each Queue and Blockers row's labels to its issue's, so the mutants "the Blockers section without the held blockers" and "a held blocker's row without claimed" are caught. Nothing held the clerk to dealing no issue labelled blocked; tests/test_team.py does now. The signature test bound a replacement with every argument only, so one that made an optional argument required, which a caller that leaves it out would meet with a TypeError, passed it; it binds the required arguments alone too. The harness's workers read a bare worker: as a worker's label, where the board needs a name after the colon (labels.holder_names), and the EagerClaimer's docstring still said it took a Given row, else the Queue. It found the template counterpart short too, below. Outside this fix it saw that the clerk deals an issue two workers' labels give to neither of them (team.is_mine), where Pick has each take it, as the board shows it to both; that is left for a pull request of its own.
- **The fourth review.** The review of the third review's folds found one thing blocking, answered here with the rest. Pick's "never an epic" closed only its Given to workers clause, so its blocker clause had a worker take an epic labelled blocker whole, where the clerk and the harness's workers take none; Pick now ends with one sentence for every section it names: never an epic, which the coordinator plans, nor an issue labelled blocked, which waits on a peer or the coordinator. Its area clause, "in your area first", read as a preference, where the Planning sentence, the clerk and the harness's workers take only an issue in the area; it says "only" now, with Planning's rule that an issue listing no files fits every area. Its reason, that a blocker comes before other work, was wrong for the work Given to workers gives, which comes first; a blocker comes before the Queue. The Labels section's todo line named one exception and so read as the whole list, where a coordinator's task, an epic carrying todo and an issue a blocker stands in the way of are not free to take either; it says such an issue is free to take as Pick says, and the blocker line reads as the Blockers paragraph does. The labels check skipped every run a fault touched, which hid a release that relabels the board before its comment and leaves it so when GitHub refuses the comment, so a held blocker's row showed it released while GitHub held its labels again; the check now skips only the rows of an issue whose putting back GitHub refused (invariants.rows_show_labels), and the search "a released blocker under every fault" catches that mutant. The lane let plausible wrong changes to the sections through, since no search seeded the mix each needs: a Blockers section that leaves out an epic, a blocked or a given blocker, a Queue that leaves out a blocker labelled todo, a row that hides epic. The lane every label mix on one issue (mutants.labels_lane, section 7) now catches each. Its first run found the oracle wrong: its Queue listed an epic labelled todo, which the library has always left out (labels.reserved) and the template's description of the board says it leaves out, while the spec said nothing of it; the spec's epic line now says the Queue leaves an epic out, and the oracle reads it (SPEC["epic"]). Outside the fix, the review found a release taking off a bare worker:, which the board, the oracle and the harness's workers read as no worker's label (labels.holder_names): coordinator.labels_released took every label that starts with a worker's prefix. It now takes only one with a name after it (labels.is_holder_label), and so does labels.names_with_prefix, so labels.worker_of no longer answers an empty name for an issue labelled worker: and worker:a; the label domain carries a bare worker: (domains.label_sets), so the labels lane holds the release's reading, while worker_of's is held by tests/test_labels.py alone, since no step of the lane shows a claim whose file is gone; and the harness's workers leave a bare worker: on when they announce a claim. Eight mutants hold these: "a release relabels the board before its comment" on the faulted blocker's search, "a row without blocked" on the released blocked task's, and, on the labels lane, "a row without epic", "the Blockers section without epic blockers", "the Blockers section without blocked blockers", "the Blockers section without the given blockers", "the Queue without the blockers labelled todo" and "a bare worker: taken off at release". It also found stale words, answered: section 7's count of the searches and its account of the held blocker's, this section's G9 row, and GUARANTEES.md's G9, which now says which runs the labels check skips and that every open blocker is listed, held or released.
- **The fifth review.** The review of those folds found nothing blocking, and these, answered here. The property a_release_takes_both_acts still read the old rule, any label that starts with a worker's prefix, so it passed under the mutant that takes a bare worker: off; it now reads a prefix with a name after it, from the prefixes and not through labels.is_holder_label, and its cases carry a bare worker:. The lane credited the faulted blocker's search with four mutants the two blocker searches without faults were added for, against what section 7 said; that search now runs after them, and the test holds each search to every mutant the lane credits it with, "a released blocked task" to "a row without blocked" too. Nothing tested that the clerk deals no epic from the shared pool, the rule Pick's "never an epic" leans on; a test of the clerk now deals an epic labelled todo and one labelled blocker to nobody (tests/test_team.py). labels.names_with_prefix repeated the bare-prefix rule where the fourth fold's commit said it used labels.is_holder_label's; one rule, labels.name_after, now serves both and labels.holder_names. Outside the fix, the review found the clerk telling a worker to carry a claim another worker holds whenever the first worker's label names the issue (team.is_mine reads only labels), and the clerk and the harness's Pick parting on an issue that two workers' labels give, 112 of the 2,048 mixes; both are the clerk's finding, which comes after F17 with a pull request of its own. And three mutants no catcher meets, each held by a unit test alone: the Overlaps landing a blocker after other work, and the clerk dealing blockers in number order with the rest, or dealing an issue labelled blocked; the clerk's finding takes them on too.
- **The choice made.** Put todo on in the release, which the spec's Release paragraph now says, rather than have the board list an issue with no label in the Queue: the label is what the clerk deals from and what a worker reading the issue sees, and a board that listed it by itself would fight an issue a person left unlabelled on purpose. Which released issues get it is the answer the owner chose on 2026-10-14 ("All but blockers") to the question the review raised: every one that carries none of todo, epic and blocker, a claim nobody queued included, which then joins the Queue for the next worker; the other answer, todo only for an issue a worker's label gave before its claim, needs the issue's history read on each such release and leaves a claim nobody queued unlisted, as it was found.
- **For the template.** At the sync of the release that carries this fix, the template's docs/agents/PROTOCOL.md takes the Labels section's todo, blocker and epic lines, the Release, Pick and Blockers paragraphs' words and the Planning sentence's, and the claim skill's step 1 the Pick paragraph's, its description, which names only "an open issue labelled todo", any issue a worker takes, and so does the symphony plugin's copy of the skill in OpenSpineConsortium/symphony-marketplace; .claude/rules/board.md's step 3 says a blocker comes before the Queue and an issue labelled blocked is taken from neither; docs/agents/routines/worker.md's step 2, which takes the lowest issue in the Queue, blocked or not, and any unclaimed blocker whatever its area, takes Pick's order: no issue labelled blocked or epic wherever it is listed, and of the blockers and the Queue only one in its area; board.yml's header says the release puts todo on; workers.yml's note that a newly queued issue wakes the workers at once says that a todo the board puts on, with the repository's own token, starts no workflow, so a released task reaches a worker in Actions at the next scheduled run; and the crowd audit (scripts/crowd.py, audit_queue), which reads only the issues labelled todo, reads those labelled blocker too, as crowd_check.QUEUE_LABELS and docs/setup/crowd/rule.md count both as queue labels. The audit goes on reporting a todo the repository's own workflow put on with a release as one no maintainer queued, and its report says the release put it on: rule.md has crowd workers take an issue, a given one included, only when a maintainer's labelled event is the latest of its todo, and a claim keeps the todo it had, so in crowd mode a release puts todo only on an issue taken without a maintainer's; a maintainer reads it and queues it again. The first plan here, to accept such a todo on an issue a maintainer had queued or given, by todo or a worker's label, was looser than rule.md, as the third review found: it would pass a todo crowd workers then refuse, and accepting every such todo would let a writer's idle claim queue an issue no maintainer queued.

**F16. A pull request GitHub calls clean while its branch is behind main never hears "main moved".** Status: fixed in the spec after 0.4.1, the library unchanged but for its words; found by the review of F9's fix, and settled by the owner's answer of 2026-10-14 ("Follow GitHub"): unlike the decision of 2026-10-13, which has the library fixed on every finding, it changes the spec, since here the library already does what the spec should say. The spec's Work in the open now says how the board reads a branch as behind: where GitHub calls its pull request behind, as it does under a rule that requires an up-to-date branch, or, where GitHub's word is blocked or draft, which says only that something else stops the merge, a rule still holding the pull request or the draft itself, where the comparison says so (F9); and that a pull request GitHub calls clean, unstable or has_hooks, a draft among them, since GitHub gives a draft the word its branch has (C0), is not held for being behind under the repository's rules, so it hears nothing however far the default branch has moved, while one GitHub calls dirty, in conflict, hears it. That is what the library has done since F9's fix (github.standing_of reads only blocked and draft through the comparison, OPAQUE_STATES), and what the template's crowd ruleset and its docs count on; the canary's C2 judges the word only where GitHub's word for the pull request it leaves behind says the spec owes it, so a copy whose ruleset does not ask for an up-to-date branch passes C2 without it (scripts/canary.py, AS_IT_STANDS). The oracle reads the new words with no switch (SPEC["behind"], SPEC["as it stands"], oracle.owes_main_moved), and the fake answers "clean" for a branch that is behind when the model's lenient_rules is on (validation/fake_github.py; model.py, whose state key holds the switch; scenarios.py, replay.py and run.py carry it). F16's trace sets it and is green as built, and one mutant reads the spec's old words (validation/mutants.py, 59 in all): "a clean word compared", which reads every word but behind and dirty through the comparison, as the first build of this answer did, and is caught on the trace at step 4 for G10, "main moved" made and not owed, and on a new search, "a draft GitHub calls clean while it is behind", which tests/test_validation_mutants.py holds, since the lane credits the mutant to the traces.
- **What happened.** F9's fix read a pull request through the comparison only where GitHub's word was blocked or draft (github.OPAQUE_STATES) and took every other word as GitHub gave it. GitHub gives "behind" only under a rule that requires a branch to be up to date before merging, as the template's main and organization rulesets do; under one that does not, as the crowd ruleset (docs/setup/ruleset-crowd.json, strict false), or with no rule at all, a pull request whose rules are met reads "clean" while its branch is behind (scripts/canary.py, C2's note), "unstable" when a check that is not required fails and "has_hooks" where a hook stands, both of which board.state_word reads as clean. So such a pull request never heard "main moved", as under 0.4.1, where the spec's words, "in whichever run finds the branch behind or conflicting", owed it the word.
- **Evidence.** validation/traces/F16.json: a claims #1 and opens its draft with lenient_rules on; a board run finds it level with main and owes nothing; main moves; the next run, GitHub's word still clean, owes it nothing and tells it nothing. It is green as built and red for G10 at step 4 under the mutant. The search "a draft GitHub calls clean while it is behind", from a claim and its draft with lenient_rules on to depth 3, finds nothing on the library and catches the mutant. tests/test_github.py, `Standing`: clean, unstable, has_hooks, behind, dirty and unknown each get no standing and no compare. tests/test_validation_model.py, the switch in the state key. tests/test_canary.py: C2 passes without the word where GitHub calls the pull request left behind clean, unstable or has_hooks, and judges it on the draft left behind, known for F9.
- **The choice made.** Follow GitHub's word rather than compare every pull request, which the first build of this answer did (github.standing_of reading every word but behind and dirty through the comparison) and its review weighed. Compared, every approved pull request in a crowd copy would hear "main moved" after each merge, though each rebase there needs a new approval and the crowd docs say to rebase a ready pull request only on a conflict (docs/setup/crowdsourcing.md, crowd/worker.md); a worker in Actions would be dealt back each ready claim behind main, a Claude run each time, though the setup docs say turning the up-to-date rule off saves those runs (docs/setup/claude-action.md); and every board run would pay one compare for each open pull request (184 calls where F9's reading took 154, over 30 drafts level with main, counted on 2026-10-14), plus the clerk's, which grow as the square of the workers that each hold a ready claim. The review also found a pull request merged during a run told "main moved", a stacked claim carried with nothing to do, and the gate's world answering any refused merge "main moved" under such a rule. Under a rule that requires an up-to-date branch GitHub says behind, so nothing changes there either way; and no work is lost under one that does not, since GitHub merges such a branch as it stands and tells a conflict as dirty. What such a rule costs is the repository's choice, not the board's: a branch behind main merges without having been tested against the main it lands on, whether or not it heard "main moved", which is why the template's main and organization rulesets ask for an up-to-date branch and only its crowd ruleset, where each push costs a new approval, does not.
- **The review.** The review of this answer, at 6183f60, found nothing blocking in the library, the oracle, the mutant or the merge, and these, each answered here. The canary's C2 expected "main moved" on the pull request it leaves behind whatever GitHub's word, so a copy whose ruleset lets a behind branch merge as it stands failed it, and its test passed C2 where the board told a pull request GitHub called clean; C2 now judges the word only where GitHub's word after the merge says the spec owes it (scripts/canary.py, owed_main_moved), and the fake site answers behind, as the canary's ruleset gives. The spec's sentence named the draft itself among what sends a pull request to the comparison, then said one GitHub calls clean may merge as it stands, which no draft may, so a draft GitHub calls clean read two ways; it now says the comparison is for the words blocked and draft, that GitHub gives a draft its branch's word, and that a pull request GitHub calls clean is not held for being behind. Nothing had read GitHub's word for a draft that is behind, on which those words rest; C2 now leaves a draft behind main beside its pull request and records the word. The gate's world over GitHub said "clean, up to date with main" of a branch GitHub calls clean while it is behind; its standing now says it does not know a clean branch is level (gate.Standing.level, GitHubWorld.standing_detail), and the gate's sentence for that clean says only that the branch merges cleanly as it stands, while git's and the memory world's clean, which is level, keeps "clean, up to date with main and merging cleanly", the words coordbench's tests of its arm read (section 3.6; the first build of this answer changed the sentence for every world, and coordbench's downstream check failed on it). Line 9 counted F16 among the items the owner decided on 2026-10-13, which it is not, and this entry did not say what a rule that does not ask for an up-to-date branch costs (the choice made, below). And the template has two things to change after all (below). Stale words went too: the library's comments_due docstring, which said every pull request behind the default branch hears so, the G10 row and the oracle's owes_main_moved, which left the draft out, the fake's, which did not say opaque_pulls wins over lenient_rules, and "always hears it" of a conflict, which a released claim's pull request never does.
- **For the template.** Its docs/agents/PROTOCOL.md says the board tells a pull request "main moved" when the default branch has moved past its branch, and its crowd and setup docs count on a pull request GitHub calls clean hearing nothing; at the sync of the release after this, its Work in the open takes the spec's new sentences, and two things change with them, which the review found. board.yml's header, which says the run comments on a pull request "that is behind it or in conflict with it", says behind as GitHub's word or, where a rule holds the pull request, the comparison says it. And docs/setup/crowd/worker.md, whose step 1 has a worker bring the default branch in whenever the board says "main moved" and whose last rules have it rebase a ready pull request only on a conflict, says which holds when: under the crowd ruleset a ready pull request awaiting its review is one a rule holds, which GitHub calls blocked, so it hears "main moved" while it is behind (F9), and a push to it after its approval costs that approval (require_last_push_approval), so a worker brings the default branch in on "main moved" while the pull request is a draft, and once it is ready only on a conflict.

**F17. A pull request from a fork whose branch bears a claim's name is taken for the claim's.** Status: fixed after 0.4.1 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14: the library fix on every finding); found by the review of F13's fix. The spec's Rules that hold everywhere now say a claim and its pull request live in the repository itself, so a pull request from a fork is no claim's, whatever its branch is called: the board lists it as a pull request of its own and tells it what it tells any other, it finishes no claim, marked ready or said finished, and a release's rename, which closes the claim's own pull request, leaves it open. The library spells the rule once, board.claim_of, the key of the claim whose pull request a pull request is, None for a branch that is no claim and for one from a fork (board.Pull.fork, from F13's review), and reads every claim's pull request through it: board.of_claim, which coordinator.is_candidate and board.is_released ask whether a ready pull request finishes the claim, and coordinator.release_text which pull request the release names; board.closed_by_release, which board.comments_due and board.pull_rows ask whether a release's rename closes a pull request; and board.claim_rows and board.open_work, which take only a claim's own pull request for the claim's. The adapter reads the same rule from the API's shapes: github.ready_branches and the drafts github.releases reads leave out a pull request from a fork (board.from_fork), so neither a ready one nor a finished draft from a fork keeps a claim from being read for its release, and a fork's draft's comments cost no call; and github.pull_detail, the clerk's read of a claim's pull request, takes the repository's own over one from a fork that the list's head filter matches, since the filter names a branch by its owner, which a fork in the same organization shares (github/docs, content/pull-requests/reference/forks.md:113: "Forks within the same organization copy collaborator and team settings from the upstream repository").
