- **What it leaves.** The table is GNU's, coreutils' through the release after 9.12 and time's through 1.10: env's short options that take a value hold the BSD ones beside GNU's, `-P altpath` of the env of FreeBSD and macOS and FreeBSD's `-L` and `-U` (a login class and a user), since those makes run the command after the value and GNU's env refuses all three and runs nothing (exit 125), so reading the next word as their value costs only the refusal of a command GNU would not have run; any other option that another make's wrapper reads and GNU's does not, or one that a later coreutils adds and that takes a value, is read as a flag, so the word after it is read as the command and a push behind it passes, as every such option did before the fix; tests/test_guard.py fails on the first machine whose coreutils' --help lists an option the table does not read as the help says it takes, where the table gains it. A command env's -S or --split-string hands on as one word, which env splits into words of its own (read since F28's fix), and the commands of sudo, xargs and the other wrappers not named here pass as before (the module's docstring).
- **For the template.** Its tools/guard_push.py finds the pushes itself, from simple_commands and push_arguments, and judges each with refusal and branches in the payload's cwd: push_arguments reads a wrapper's options through launch and past_options, so the wrappers' reading, long_option_of, long_value_is_next and the table, reaches its hook when the template vendors the release that carries the fix, and a push behind `env --uns HOME` or `nice --adj 5` is then refused as anywhere; env_directory's, names_chdir and the directory `env --ch` names, reaches it only once that shim calls guard.refused_push, as F21's does, since until then it judges every push in the session's clone. The plugin marketplace runs guard.py's own run, which takes the whole fix with the release.

**F27. A label GitHub takes off and loses its answer to is not put back.** Status: fixed after 0.5.0 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14: the library fix on every finding); found by the first review of F23's fix, its finding 4, which judged it older than that fix and of the same family as F15's todo and F23's comment. A release takes claimed and every worker's label off its issue one DELETE at a time (take_label_off), and a run that fails there puts back what it took off (SPEC["put back"]). Until the fix the labels it put back were those GitHub had answered with success: the first except of release_one put back taken_off alone, and take_label_off raises on any answer but a 404. A server error or no answer at all may come after GitHub made the write, as wrote_nothing says of the release comment since F23 and put_back of the todo since F15's review, so a label GitHub took off and lost its answer to stayed off while the others went back: the held claim's issue carried worker:<name> without claimed, or, where the worker's label was the one lost, claimed without it, against Announce's labels naming the holder alone, until the next run released the claim or its holder's push kept it with the labels so. The fix reads the removal as the release reads its comment: take_labels_off returns, beside the failure, the labels to put back, the one whose removal failed among them unless wrote_nothing(problem) says GitHub refused it before acting, a 4xx other than a rate limit's on a try that followed none refused for a rate limit. The rendered line changes only in what it lists: "#N: not released, taking the labels off failed: ..." now ends "; claimed put back" where GitHub answered claimed's removal with a server error or no answer, where it ended at the error before, and lists the failed label beside the ones taken off, as in "; claimed, worker:nightly put back"; after a refusal it reads as before.
- **Evidence.** validation/traces/F27.json: a claims #1, b #2, c #3 and d #4, an hour apart; at 25 hours a board run under label-delete-lost, GitHub taking claimed off #1 and answering 502, leaves #1 labelled claimed, todo and worker:a, with no comment, the branch kept and the claim held, and the next run releases it whole; at 26 hours the same under label-delete-unanswered, the connection timing out with claimed taken off #2; at 27 hours under label-delete-422 GitHub refuses to take claimed off #3, and the run puts back nothing, the issue as it was; at 28 hours, under label-delete-limited-then-404, GitHub refuses claimed's removal from #4 with a 429 though it takes the label off, answers the client's second try 404 and worker:d's removal 500, and the run puts back both, the 404 following a try that may have taken claimed off (take_label_off's retried_404_taken; the fix's review); each next run releases the claim whole. On main's library at c6e8e1f, under this fix's harness, the trace is red at steps 16, 18 and 22 for G3L ("#1: the release failed and its labels were not put back"), the issues left with todo and worker:a, todo and worker:b, and todo and worker:d, the first two as the review's reproduction found on 4c5e3f2. It is red under the two mutants of the fix, which the lane credits to the regression traces (validation/mutants.py, 156 in all): "a label taken off and lost stays off" (take_labels_off never putting back the label whose removal failed, the rule as built until the fix) at steps 16, 18 and 22, "a label refused its removal put back" (putting it back whatever GitHub answered) at steps 20 and 22, and, from the review, "a 404 after a rate limit read as never on" (take_label_off reading every 404 as a label the issue never carried, the fix as first built) at step 22, through the check the fix adds beside labels_put_back, invariants.put_back_unasked, which holds a failed release to putting back only a label the run took off or may have taken off by the calls' own answers (labels_may_be_off, may_have_written, read from the fault and never from the library's rule). tests/test_github.py: a second label answered 502 puts both back, one refused with a 403 that is no rate limit's puts back the first alone, the first answered with no answer at all puts it back, the first refused with a 422 puts back nothing and posts no comment, and, from the review, the first refused 429 and answered 404 on the client's second try counts as taken off, so a 502 on the second puts both back, while taking a todo off again reads such a 404 as off.
- **The choice made.** Put back the label whose removal failed after any answer that may come after GitHub took it off, rather than read the issue again to see whether it is still on: putting back a label still on is a POST GitHub answers 200 to and changes nothing, so the put-back is right either way and costs no read, where a read could itself fail and would need a rule of its own. After a refusal that says GitHub took nothing off the label is not put back, since it is still on, and putting it back is a write the release does not owe, which after a 404 would put on a label the issue never carried and whose own failure would say "putting claimed back failed too, so they may still be off" of a label still on. The same answer is read the same way for the todo a release puts on (F15's review: put_labels_on hands put_back the todo whatever GitHub answered, and a 404 to taking it off again counts as off) and for the release comment (F23), so the three steps of a release now read a failed write alike. F22's freeing puts nothing back by design, every step bringing the issue nearer to free, so a removal or a todo GitHub made and lost its answer to is the freeing's progress and leaves the issue for the next run to read: nothing changes there.
- **What it costs.** Nothing on a run GitHub answers; on one whose label removal fails with a server error or no answer, the put-back POST names one label more, in the one call it made already where an earlier label came off, and one call where none had.
- **What it leaves.** A label whose removal GitHub refused for a rate limit on the client's last try, or after a try it refused so, is read as one GitHub may have taken off, as F23's review reads the comment, so it is put back, harmlessly where GitHub had not acted. A 404 after such a try is read the same way, since the refused try may have taken the label off (the fix's review, against which the first build read the 404 as a label never on, as a plain 404 is, and left claimed off a held claim's issue when a later label failed); the release goes on there, the label being off either way, and counts it among those it took off, so it goes back only should a later step fail, and goes back too where someone else took it off between the board's read and the release, as a label the board read on the issue. A put-back POST that itself fails, its answer lost or GitHub refusing it, leaves the labels as F15 and F20 leave them, said on the line, until a run releases the claim or a person puts them back. The second label's own fault, GitHub losing its answer to the worker's label after claimed came off, is tested in tests/test_github.py and not traced, since the fake's fault takes the first DELETE of the run.
- **For the template.** Its docs/agents/PROTOCOL.md takes the spec's new clause in Release at its vendoring of the release after 0.5.0.

**F28. The push guard reads only a shell name's assignment after env, and env's -S string as one word.** Status: fixed after 0.5.0 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, section 14: the library fix on every finding); found by the adversarial review of F26's fix. GNU env reads its options with getopt_long, stopping at the first word that is no option; then, in coreutils' env.c, takes a lone - as -i, and then every word that holds a = (its loop on strchr(argv[optind], '=')) as an assignment it puts in the command's environment, before the command; the env of FreeBSD and macOS reads name=value the same way. bash takes only a name and a = before a command as an assignment, and runs any other word as the command, so `1x=2 git push` runs nothing. The guard read the words after env's options with the shell's ASSIGNMENT, so `env 1x=2 git push --force origin main`, which env runs, was read as running 1x=2. GNU env's -S, and --split-string or a start of it, splits its string into words by rules of its own (build_argv) and reads them in the option's place, as options again, so `env -S 'git push origin main'` runs the push, where the guard read the string as -S's value, one word. And `env GIT_DIR=x` with no command prints env's environment and sets nothing in the shell, where the guard read it, as a lone `GIT_DIR=x`, as the shell naming its repository, so every later push in the command was judged by its words and in the session's own clone alone. The fix reads env's words as env reads them: after its options, a lone - is passed (guard.past_lone_dash) and every word that holds a = is an assignment (guard.env_assigns, guard.past_env_assignments), counted with the shell's, so GIT_DIR or GIT_REFERENCE_BACKEND given there names the repository as on the push; each -S string is split as env.c splits it (guard.split_string) and its words put in its place before env's options are read (guard.with_split_strings, Launch.argv, which push_arguments, chdir_paths and moved_to read); and an assignment of a command that runs nothing sets the shell's own variable only where no wrapper stands before it but bash's time, which times the assignment in the shell (guard.sets_the_shells_own). After any other wrapper the shell's reading stays: nice, timeout, nohup, command and exec run a word that holds a = as the command. No rendered string changes.
- **What happened.** Until the fix `env 1x=2 git push origin main`, `env A-B=1 ...`, `env =x ...`, `env -i -- A.B=1 ...`, `nice env x-y=1 ...`, `env --uns FOO 1x=2 ...`, `env -- - git push origin main` and `env -S 'git push origin main'` passed the guard, where env 9.4 runs the command each names, as `env 1x=2 printf ran` shows; and from a session's clone that never fetched a's claim, `env GIT_DIR=x; git -C ../other push --force-with-lease origin claude/1` passed a lease git takes in ../other, which had fetched it.
- **Evidence.** validation/traces/F28.json: a claims #1; the guard on `env 1x=2 git push origin main`, `env -- - A.B=1 git push origin +claude/7`, `nice env x-y=1 git push origin HEAD:board` and `env -S 'git push origin main'`, each a command of the guard grammar judged by git's recorded answer, each refused; and b's leases from its session's clone onto a's claim from b-other, by `env 1x=2 git -C ../b-other`, inside `env -S` and after `env GIT_DIR=<the clones>/b/.git` alone (verbs.ELSEWHERE's env-assigned, env-split and env-alone), each refused, a still holding the claim at the board; green since the fix, in memory and over git, and, on the guard of main at fcbe822, red for G11a at steps 1 to 4, for G11a and G1 at steps 5 to 7, each lease taken, and for G1 at the board, step 8; and red under the four mutants of the fix, which the lane credits to the regression traces (validation/mutants.py, 176 in all): "env's assignments read as the shell's" (env_assigns as ASSIGNMENT) at step 1, "env's lone - not read" at step 2, "env's -S string not split" (split_string refusing every string) at step 4, and "env's lone assignment read as the shell's" (sets_the_shells_own always) at step 7. The guard grammar holds the new shapes (section 8.3): 52,192 commands, 628 of the new 932's forbidden pushes let through by main's guard, none by the fix's. tests/test_guard.py: `EnvAssignmentTest` (each shape refused, behind nice, after --uns with its value, -C and -i, in a bundle -iS and through --split-string and --sp; the harmless forms passed, `env FOO=bar make`, `env -u X ls`, `1x=2 git push origin main`, which bash runs as 1x=2, and `env FOO=1 - git push`, which env runs as -; GIT_DIR after env's other assignments, or in its -S string, naming the repository; env GIT_DIR=x alone setting nothing, where a lone assignment and one after time set the shell's, as bash shows; split_string held to the env installed here on 30 strings, quotes, \_, \c, comments and the strings env refuses among them; each of 23 env word lists run before printf, the guard reading printf as the command wherever env runs it, and reading on past words env refuses, exit 125; and each mutant letting its shape through) and `OtherCloneTest` (`env 1x=2 git -C ../other push origin HEAD` refused as a push of other's main, and the three leases refused, a dry run showing git would take a's claim, each passed under its mutant); tests/test_validation_guard_grammar.py holds the 157 of each new row.
- **The choice made.** Read the -S string by env.c's rules, in the guard, rather than leave it one word as bash -c's is: env splits it itself, by rules that need no shell (quotes, a few escapes, a comment, ${NAME}), so the guard can read the words env runs exactly, where what bash -c runs needs a second shell's reading. A ${NAME} is kept as written, as the guard keeps the shell's own $NAME, since its value is the command's environment's; a string env refuses (a quote that never closes, a $ that opens no ${NAME}, an escape it does not know, \c in double quotes) stays one word, since env then runs nothing, and the words after it are read as before the fix. A word with a = is read as an assignment only after env, since bash, nice, timeout, nohup, command and exec run it as the command; the shell's own reading before a command is unchanged.
- **What it costs.** Nothing on a command with no env; behind one, a look at each word after its options for a =, and for an -S string one pass over it, each word list rebuilt once per -S.
- **What it leaves.** A ${NAME} in an -S string that holds the command's own words, as `env -S '${G} push origin main'`, is read as the word ${NAME}, as `$G push origin main` is in the shell, so such a push passes, and one whose ${NAME} is unset, which env drops, may shift the words after it. An assignment env makes to GIT_DIR+ (env's name for `GIT_DIR+=x`, which the shell would read as appending) is read as one to GIT_DIR, as before, so such a push is judged by its words and in the session's own clone too, a reading more than it needs. `/usr/bin/time GIT_DIR=x` alone, which runs GIT_DIR=x as a command and sets nothing, is read as bash's time, setting it. FreeBSD's and macOS's env take -S and read name=value as GNU's does; their own differences in -S's escapes, if any, are not read.
- **For the template.** Its tools/guard_push.py finds the pushes from simple_commands and push_arguments, which read env's words through launch, so env's assignments, its lone - and its -S reach its hook when the template vendors the release that carries the fix; sets_the_shells_own reaches it once that shim calls guard.refused_push, as F21's does. The plugin marketplace runs guard.py's own run, which takes the whole fix with the release.

## 14. Build order and the owner's decisions

Pull requests, in order:
1. **Foundations, in memory:** the sound state key, waits of 24 and 25 hours and the atomic verbs; no pruning at a violation; the effect-equivalence oracle built from the spec's text, and G15; validation/model.py, fake_github.py, cycle.py, verbs.py, policies.py, oracle.py and invariants.py, GUARANTEES.md and its test. Built, with explore.py, page.py and the fake's transport (an ASCII request line, Link pagination, `mergeable` null on the first read of a run) and the board-branch write that happens only when a run exits 0, which were step 3's: validation/README.md says what each file holds and what it measured. Until known.json (step 5), the open findings were switches in the oracle, `oracle.AS_BUILT`, which the file's `as_built` now feeds, and F7 shows only under the careless scope.
2. **The mutation lane,** standard library, 10 to 20 mutants, as the acceptance test of step 1: no step after this one starts until every mutant is caught. Built: validation/mutants.py, 19 mutants at that step, every one caught (validation/README.md says by which search, and what the lane made the harness add); validate.yml runs the lane, and step 6 adds the deep lane to it.
3. **The run as the workflow makes it:** the concurrency group, with its pending run replaced; and the board run in two halves in cli.py. Built: `cli.plan_board` and `cli.act_on_board`, which the next release carries to the consumers, with `board_live_run` unchanged in what it does; `cycle.start_run` and `cycle.finish_run`, the scope's `split_board`, and the checks judging a split run by the states its halves read (section 3.3); the events each verb fires and the queue in the model (`RepoModel.fire` and `starts`), the scope's `events` now naming which the model hears (section 3.3); S09 and F12's mechanism as tests; the twentieth mutant and the lane's fifth search, the run split (section 7). The review of this step made the queue GitHub's (a run into an idle group starts and is never cancelled, so F12 needs a run in progress), had the first half judged when it is made, so a search judges a first half at its last level as it judges a whole run, and met the faults on every run that can start; its verification then had the run queued start before anything else, kept the skipped push as a run where the scope splits runs, since it holds the group, and had the second half's writes judged by position, whatever their kind. Measured: two workers on one issue to depth 4 reach 337 states hearing every event, each act's run forced before the next act, 4,102 with the run split, and 974 with both, in under 3 seconds each, with no violation on 0.4.1; the lane takes about 100 seconds.
4. **The fast lane, under 60 seconds:** S01 to S05 and the three table-driven tests (section 5.3); the checker at depth 5 plus searches from scenario prefixes; the guard grammar against git's answers, which this step records first, into validation/guard_git.json (section 8.2), by the command the deep lane later runs again; dealing; the Unicode-class property lane. Built: validation/scenarios.py and run.py (the eleven scenarios with their variants, every one green as built, F7 red on the careless one, F2 and F12 red where named with their switches off; all in the memory world: the git runs of S01 and S05 and the variant S05g, with git_store.py, wait for step 6, since a board run over git costs 30 to 70 ms, section 3.4); guard_grammar.py and guard_git.json (699 argument lists recorded on git 2.43.0 in 37 seconds, 20,970 commands judged in 1.6, no readable command let through, nothing refused that the rules allow; the review of this step found the lease-outside-claude/ rule had no offer to fire on and gave the worlds a diverged branch that is no claim); dealing.py and clerk.py (54 deals, none doubled from one look, F4 from differing looks); properties.py, domains.py and unproven.json (21 properties, 6,763 names, about a second; the review had the properties restate the spec's words, not the library's constants, and the lane refuse a property written as asserts alone); tests/test_validation_tables.py (S06, S10, S11) and tests/test_validation_fast_lane.py (depth 5 in 11 seconds, 29,531 states; E-three at depth 4; E-prefix at depth 3 from the design's points; every fault kind at depth 3); five more mutants, 25 in all, with two lanes and a sixth search, all caught; scripts/validate.py. The reductions of 6.4 were measured unnecessary at depth 5 (pruning no-op waits gave 5 percent) and are left for the deep lane; model.clone became a one-level copy, 1.6 times faster. The fast lane's tests take 27 seconds on a laptop; validation/README.md has the rest.
5. **Regression traces for F1 to F12,** each red in known.json until its fix lands, which then removes the entry; F1, F3, F5, F6, F10 and F11, fixed in 0.4.1, are recorded green from the start. Built, with traces for F13 and F14 too: validation/traces/F1.json to F14.json, the table of 5.3 as files of steps in scenarios.py's shape with the expectations spelled out (validation/replay.py plays one through run.py, which gained the two steps no verb covers, the guard judged by git's recorded answer and the clerk's deal from the model as the steps left it); validation/known.json and known.py, the one record of what is red on purpose, whose `as_built` is now where the oracle takes its switches from (F2, F9, F12, F13 and F14 at that step; none now, every one fixed since, F2 the last), with sixteen entries: the nine red steps of the traces (F2, F9, F12, F13, F14 with their switch off; F4 and F7 as built) and the seven of the scenarios; four mutants that undo fixes of 0.4.1 (F6's sort key and F6's quoting of the request path, F10's release of a finished draft by hand, F11's refused comment), and the traces as the mutation lane's first and cheapest catcher, which also catches F1's, F3's and F5's mutants on their traces; `python3 scripts/validate.py traces` and `replay <file>`. The traces play in a third of a second, every expectation holds, and tests/test_validation_traces.py holds each trace's red results to known.json as built and with each switch off in turn, and each fixed finding's trace red under its mutant at the step the table names. The library is unchanged. Two readings the table did not have: F10's trace under its mutant is red for G5, G3 and G10, as the invariants attribute the page, the rename and the comment, where the table said G9; and F6's trace under either of its mutants is red for G15, the run exiting 1, where the table said G9 for the render, since a render that raises is a run that fails. F12's trace takes the table's route, the merge's four events, where the test of step 3 took the worker's skipped push; with the switch off both runs after the merge are red, since the spec owes the word in any run.
6. **The deep lane:** two issues at depth 7, one issue at depth 8 with the depth-aware digest set, the soak, conformance against git, the git world (git_store.py) with S01, S05 and S05g run over it, git's answers for the guard grammar recorded again on every deep run, and the gate over BareGitWorld; validate.yml. Built: validation/git_store.py, the git world, with run.play taking a world and every scenario and trace playing over git with the results known.json records for memory (S01 shows git's "fetch first"; on S01c and F7's trace git would take the lease after a fetch, and the guard, judging the push in the worker's clone with the library's own claim-file reader, refuses it as in memory); validation/deep.py, the depth-first search with the digest set, E-two, E-deep, E-faults and E-activity from the fast lane's start, no reduction applied (the owner's rule, correctness over cost), every violation shortened by validation/shrink.py and written as a replayable trace file, conformance over the counterexamples and 200 sampled paths comparing what both worlds left after every step, and the guard grammar's answers recorded again and compared; validation/soak.py, 1,000 seeded walks of 200 steps; validation/gate_search.py, G13 to depth 8 under four policies over MemoryWorld, S05g over BareGitWorld and forty seeded sequences over a bare repository per sequence; validation/lanes.py and `scripts/validate.py deep`, the parts in parallel processes, one forked process with a pipe per part so a process that dies takes its part alone and the parent forks with no helper thread, with deep.json rewritten whole as each ends, saying whether the run finished, and each search checkpointed every quarter of an hour under a stamp of the search, so `--resume` continues a run a restart killed, a stopped search, a stopped gate exploration, a part that raised and the conformance of counterexamples written since included, and only that run (other settings, another git or other code, the harness's data files counted as code, are refused, as is an out folder among that code, another search's checkpoint is started over, and a second run into a folder where one is in progress is refused); validation/known.json's `searches`, where E-faults' meeting with F7 was recorded until F7's fix; validate.yml's deep job, weekly, by hand and on the label "deep validation", uploading validation-out. Measured on 4 processors: the whole run in 9,559 s (2 h 39 min) at commit acc83fb, green: E-deep at depth 8, 16,706,180 states in 7,563 s (2 h 6 min), no violation; E-two at depth 7, 18,787,930 states in 9,494 s (2 h 38 min), no violation; E-faults at depth 4, 41,415 states in 92 s (2 min), no violation; E-activity at depth 5, 1,035,026 states in 1,040 s (17 min), no violation; the soak, 1,000 walks, 582 s of walking, no violation; the gate, 51,424 to 63,780 states a policy in memory, 40 sequences over git, no violation; conformance, 200 paths, 0 counterexamples, 0 disagreements, 204 s; the guard, 699 lists, 0 differing, 45 s; the git world, 11 scenarios and 14 traces matching known.json in 16.9 and 11.3 s. Main's F15, F16 and F17 came in after that run; at the final code, commit 01afc41, every part but the four searches ran again, green, in 443 s (validation/runs/2026-10-14-merged/). The library is unchanged. validation/README.md, step 6, has the rest.
7. **One GET now for F9 (canary C0),** made by hand on 2026-10-14 on two live drafts, from which F9 is fixed (sections 11 and 13). Then the monthly canary on the canary repository, once it carries the current template: canary.yml and scripts/canary.py. Built: scripts/canary.py, the checks C0 to C7 as functions over one site object, with their records, the summary and the failure issue; .github/workflows/canary.yml, monthly and by hand, minting the App's token with Actions read when the App has it and without when not; tests/test_canary.py, every check end to end against a fake site, the git side against a bare repository; the design's test repository replaced by OpenSpineConsortium/symphony-protocol-canary throughout. The review's answers and the session of 2026-10-14 finished it: the committer-date test compares instants, since git 2.55 on GitHub's runners prints a UTC date with Z where git 2.43 prints +00:00; canary.Site's REST side is tested over the library's FakeOpener, method by method; the run counts have their negative tests, a cancelled push-to-main run and a run the board's own push started; and, decided that day, the canary reads an expectation that fails for a finding fixed on main after the version the canary repository vendors as known (known.json's `fixed_after`, F7 and F12 after 0.4.1), so C2 runs monthly from the first run with its "main moved" judged by the state, rather than waiting for the release. The second review's answers that day: the run stops its checks at the token's hour and on a 401; C2 judges the word only once the check on the pull request left behind passed, and waits for the push to main's run to finish; C6 reads updated_at after each of the comment and the push, and asks nothing that depends on a rename that never came; each missed expectation is marked known or not by itself; and the run takes the canary repository alone unless told. The first run waits on the owner: the adoption through the front door, and Actions read on SymphonyBoard for the run counts.
8. **The Markdown report** last; the HTML page optional.

The owner's decisions:
- **F2.** Which identity for a piece of open work in the overlap marker: the issue for as long as the claim lives, so opening the pull request tells nothing again, or the pull request once there is one, with the issue's markers read as told too? Decided 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every item), the coordinator's checkpoint of 2026-10-14 recording the choice as the issue staying the work's identity for the whole claim: the issue names the piece in the marker and is where it is told, its claim's own pull requests' markers counting too, and each claim on the issue in turn; built, section 13.
- **F12.** Make "main moved" depend on state, telling on any run each behind or conflicting pull request with no marker for main's current sha; or add `queue: max` to the board's concurrency group in the copies' board.yml. Decided 2026-10-13: both; built, section 13.
- **F4.** Should one job deal the whole run and pass the deals to the matrix, or is the occasional wasted run accepted, since the lock still holds? Decided 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every item): one job deals the whole run; built, section 13.
- **F7.** Which second fence, if any: the guard reading the claim file on `origin/claude/N` before a lease push, the board reading the activity API's `force_push` events, or neither? A ruleset blocking forced pushes on `claude/**` is ruled out: it refuses the prescribed lease and makes renames an administrator's. Decided 2026-10-13 (the owner: fix whatever is broken, the library fix on every item): the guard reads the claim file; built, section 13.
- **F8.** Keep the committer date, as the spec says, and state the trust it places in the pusher; or read the server's `timestamp` of the push from the activity API, once canary C5 shows it behaves as documented. Decided 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every item): the push's time, the committer date only where the activity gives none; C5 passed on 2026-10-15; built, section 13.
- **F9.** After C0's one GET: should drafts hear "main moved", read the way GitHubWorld reads them? Decided 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every item): a draft hears "main moved" like any other pull request; C0 was read on 2026-10-14; built, section 13.
- **F13.** Order the overlaps by when the work began, or write the number order into the spec? Decided 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every item): by when the work began, as GitHub records the push that made its branch; built, section 13.
- **F14.** Show every open, unclaimed issue a worker's label names under Given to workers, or write todo, and the epics' exclusion, into the spec? Decided 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every item): show every one; built, section 13.
- **F15.** Found by the review of F14's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): a release puts todo on an issue that carries none of todo, epic and blocker; built, section 13. The fix's review asked which released issues should get todo; the recommended answer, built here, is every one that carries none of the three, a claim nobody queued included, and the owner chose it on 2026-10-14. Its second review had a worker reading the board take an unclaimed blocker before the Queue, as the clerk deals one, since a released blocker carries no todo, and its third made Pick's words say what the clerk and the harness's workers do and the lane read a held blocker's row; its fourth ended Pick with one rule for every section, never an epic nor an issue labelled blocked, had the lane read every mix of labels on one issue, and left a bare worker: on at a release; built, section 13.
- **F16.** Found by the review of F9's fix: does a pull request GitHub calls clean while its branch is behind main, as under the crowd ruleset or under no rule, hear "main moved", as the spec's words had it? Its first build read every such pull request through the comparison; its review found that would have every approved crowd pull request rebase and need approval again after each merge, and workers in Actions carry ready claims the setup docs say a lenient rule spares. The owner was asked on 2026-10-14 and chose the recommended answer, built here, which follows GitHub's word: such a pull request is not held for being behind and hears nothing, which the spec now says and the library already does. Its review had the canary's C2 judge the word only where it is owed and read a draft left behind; section 13.
- **F17.** Found by the review of F13's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): a pull request from a fork whose branch bears a claim's name was taken for the claim's, so a stranger's ready one kept the claim from ever being released; a pull request from a fork is now no claim's, whatever its branch is called (board.claim_of); built, section 13.
- **F18.** Found by the fifth review of F15's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): the clerk read whose a claim is, and whose a task is, from the issue's labels alone, so a worker whose label an issue carried was told to carry another worker's claim, a claim that no label or two labels named was carried by nobody, and a task the labels of two workers give was dealt to neither; a claim is now the worker's its claim file names, and such a task goes to the first of them in the team's order with nothing else to do (team.Snapshot.holder, team.moves_from); built, section 13.
- **F19.** Found by the review of F17's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): a pull request someone who may not write opened from a claim's own branch, as GitHub's documentation says anyone who may read the repository can, was taken for the claim's, so a stranger's ready one kept the claim from ever being released and its finished draft held it; a claim's pull request is now one a writer or an App opened (claims.opened_by_writer), the adapter marks a stranger's (github.mark_strangers) and every reading goes through board.claim_of, and a release's rename closes it with the claim's own (board.closed_by_release); built, section 13.
- **F20.** Found by the review of build step 2 (symphony-protocol#16), under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding; the handoffs call it the stuck claim): a run whose release comment GitHub refused, and the read of the issue's comments after it too, put the labels back beside a comment that might stand, which held the claim for good; such a run now leaves the labels as the release left them and counts no release, and the next run settles it (github.unsettled); built, section 13, with the version raised to 0.5.0, whose release waits for the owner's approval.
- **F21.** Found by the review behind the taxonomy of 2026-10-15, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding), and given its own pull request by the owner's handoff of 2026-10-15: the push guard judged every push in the session's own clone, so a lease onto another worker's claim sent by `git -C` or a `cd` from a clone that had fetched it passed; each push is now judged in each clone it may run in (guard.refused_push, guard.push_places), the one it runs in and, where the guard cannot tell that the shell made a move or which clone it is, or a variable names the repository, also where the command starts or in the session's own, after 0.5.0, so the release after it carries the fix; built, section 13, with four reviews.
- **F22.** Found by the same review, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding), and given its own pull request by the owner's handoff of 2026-10-15: an issue reopened once its claim ended kept the labels that claim left, so with no branch standing it was listed in no section and dealt to no worker, and with the merged branch kept, in-review kept that claim from ever being released; the coordinator now frees an open issue GitHub says was reopened, in the board's run before it releases (coordinator.reopened_due, coordinator.freeing, github.free_reopened), todo on and the claim's labels off, claimed last, where no branch stands, and in-review alone where one does, after 0.5.0, so the release after it carries the fix; built, section 13. Its review settled the question this line left the owner, under the same decision: a released branch reads as a release being undone only where its release came after the issue's last close, as the issue's events date it (github.ended_releases), so a later claim's merge ends the earlier claim's too, and since its second review where the label claimed went on after that close, a person's undo, and since its third later than the newest entry the activity lists for the claim branch since the close, which a claim made after the close puts its own claimed on before (section 13, F22).
- **F23.** Found by the second review of F20's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): a release comment GitHub answered with a 502 or no answer at all, written but listed only after the run's read of the issue's comments, stood beside the labels the run put back, which held the claim for good; such a run now leaves the labels as the release left them, as where it cannot read the comments, and puts them back only after a refusal, a 4xx other than a rate limit's since its review, whose third finding found the rule resting on C4 (github.wrote_nothing, github.not_listed); built, section 13, after 0.5.0, whose version it keeps.
- **F25.** Found in the reviews after F21's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): the push guard read a command whole or not at all, so a quote that never closes hid every push in the command and an apostrophe in a here-document's body could pair with one in a later body around the push between them; the hook now reads each command whole, leniently, and with its bodies cut out, and judges every push either reading finds (guard.readings, guard.here_document_bodies), in a time in proportion to the command, refusing unread one whose reading runs past 10 seconds, since Claude Code reads a hook that times out as no objection (guard.TOO_SLOW), after 0.5.0, so the release after it carries the fix; built, section 13.
- **F26.** Found by the fourth review of F21's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): the push guard read a wrapper's long option only by its whole name, so the push behind `env --uns HOME`, `nice --adj 5` or `timeout --sig KILL 60` passed and `env --ch ../other` hid the clone a push runs in; each wrapper's long options are now read as GNU's getopt_long reads them (guard.long_option_of, guard.long_value_is_next, guard.names_chdir), after 0.5.0, so the release after it carries the fix; built, section 13.
- **F27.** Found by the first review of F23's fix, its finding 4, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): a label GitHub took off and answered with a server error or no answer at all, as a release took it off, was not put back with the labels the run had taken off, so a held claim's issue lost claimed or its worker's label until the next run; the run now puts back the label whose removal failed too, unless GitHub refused it with a 4xx other than a rate limit's (github.take_labels_off); built, section 13, after 0.5.0, whose version it keeps.
- **F28.** Found by the adversarial review of F26's fix, under the owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the library fix on every finding): the push guard read after env's options only what the shell reads as an assignment, the lone - GNU env takes as -i as the command, env's -S string as one word, and `env GIT_DIR=x` alone as GIT_DIR set in the shell, so the push behind `env 1x=2`, `env -- -` or inside `env -S '...'` passed; env's words are now read as env reads them (guard.env_assigns, guard.past_lone_dash, guard.split_string, guard.sets_the_shells_own), after 0.5.0, so the release after it carries the fix; built, section 13.
- **The canary.** Adopting symphony-protocol-canary through the front door, with Symphony Setup installed on it alone and "Do not share" ticked; for C2 and C6's run counts, Actions read added to SymphonyBoard, which the workflow asks for and does without until then; and, for C7, the owner's own sign-in from a clone, or Symphony Setup's token, for the run.
